\Gamma_{k,\ell}(x,\deg F)=\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{1-n_{r}}.

Proof.

The rational function statement follows from a general property of Kubota
L
-series
[
43
, bottom of page 245]
. The functional equations of the Kubota
L
-series
[
43
, top of page 251]
imply, for all
k
:

\begin{split}&D(x,\vec{f},k)=\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{\deg F+1-(\deg F+1-2k)\%n_{r}}\frac{1-q}{1-q\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n_{r}}}D\left(\frac{g^{2}}{q^{2}x},\vec{f},k\right)\\
&+(\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{\ell(1+\deg F)}(-1)g_{(\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{2k-\deg F-1}}\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{\deg F+1}\frac{1-\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{-n_{r}}}{1-q\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n_{r}}}D\left(\frac{g^{2}}{q^{2}x},\vec{f},\ell\right)\end{split}

(3.9)

where
\ell=1+\deg F-k
. Rewriting these functional equations in vector form gives the desired statement. The special entries of the scattering matrix come from adding the two terms together.
∎

Note that there is a slight discrepancy between the functional equation given in
[
43
]
and the functional equation (
3.9
) as written: the power of
(\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})(-1)
does not match. This is because of a subtlety in the definition of the Kubota
L
-series. Patterson uses a different additive character from ours, so that his function field Gauss sum
g(r,\varepsilon,c)
(with underlying character
\chi
of order
n
) is our
\chi(-1)^{\deg c}g_{\chi}(r,c)
. This leads to a small difference in the functional equation.

Importantly for us,
\Gamma_{k,\ell}(x,\deg F)
depends on
\deg F\bmod n_{r}
, and the full functional equation depends on
\deg F
, but not on
f_{1},\ldots f_{r-1}
themselves.

The local generating series
D_{\pi}(x,\vec{f},k)
satisfy corresponding local functional equations.

Proposition 3.2
.

For
v_{\pi}(f_{1}\cdots f_{r-1})\leq 1
,
D_{\pi}(x,\vec{f},k)
is a rational function of
x
with denominator dividing
1-q^{-\deg\pi}\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n_{r}\deg\pi}
. We have the vector functional equation:

\left(D_{\pi}(x,\vec{f},k)\right)_{k}=\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{v_{\pi}(F)\deg\pi}\left(\left(\frac{F^{(\pi)}}{\pi}\right)_{\xi\chi^{M_{rr}}}^{k+\ell}\Gamma_{\pi,k,\ell}(x,v_{\pi}F)\right)_{k,\ell}\left(D_{\pi}\left(\frac{g^{2}}{q^{2}x},\vec{f},\ell\right)\right)_{\ell}

(3.10)

where
\left(\Gamma_{\pi,k,\ell}(x,v_{\pi}F)\right)_{k,\ell}
is the
n_{r}\times n_{r}
scattering matrix with

\begin{split}\Gamma_{\pi,k,k}(x,v_{\pi}F)=&\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{(1-(v_{\pi}F+1-2k)\%n_{r})\deg\pi}\frac{1-q^{-\deg\pi}}{1-q^{-\deg\pi}\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n_{r}\deg\pi}}\end{split}

\begin{split}\Gamma_{\pi,k,\ell}(x,v_{\pi}F)=&(\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{\ell(1+v_{\pi}F))\deg\pi}(-1)\frac{g_{(\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{2k-v_{\pi}F-1}}(1,\pi)}{q^{\deg\pi}}\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{\deg\pi}\frac{1-\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{-n_{r}\deg\pi}}{1-q^{-\deg\pi}\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n_{r}\deg\pi}}\end{split}

for
\ell\equiv 1+v_{\pi}F-k\bmod n_{r}
, and all other entries equal to
0
. In addition, there are special entries in the matrix when
k\equiv\ell\equiv 1+v_{\pi}F-k\bmod n_{r}
. In this case

\Gamma_{\pi,k,\ell}(x,v_{\pi}F)=\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{(1-n_{r})\deg\pi}.

Proposition
3.2
can be checked by direct computation from the evaluations of
D_{\pi}(x,\vec{f},k)
above.

We remark that the local scattering matrix
\left(\Gamma_{\pi,k,\ell}(x,d)\right)_{k,\ell}
is obtained from the global scattering matrix
\left(\Gamma_{k,\ell}(x,d)\right)_{k,\ell}
by the same change of variables which relates the axiomatic local and global multiple Dirichlet series coefficients. The fact that
x\mapsto\frac{g^{2}}{q^{2}x}
commutes with this change of variables is crucial to the proof below.

The next Lemma gives the inverse of the scattering matrix. This lemma is not used in the rest of this section, but is used in the proof of Theorem
5.6
.

Lemma 3.3
.

We have the identity

(\Gamma(x,K))^{-1}=\Gamma(\frac{g^{2}}{q^{2}x},K).

The formula should hold because, when we apply the functional equation twice, we transform a multiple Dirichlet series to itself. The lemma does not follow immediately from the functional equation (
3.8
) because we lose information when we multiply by a vector. Instead we verify the lemma by a direct computation.

Proof.

The matrix
\Gamma(x,K)
is conjugate under a permutation matrix to a block diagonal matrix with
2\times 2
and
1\times 1
blocks. The
1\times 1
blocks have unique entry
\left(\frac{qx}{g)}\right)^{1-n_{i}}
with inverse
\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n_{i}-1}
which can be obtained from
\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{1-n_{i}}
by the substitution
x\mapsto\frac{g}{q^{2}x^{2}}
.

The two-by-two block consisting of the columns and rows
k,l
where
k+l\equiv 1+K\bmod n_{i}
has determinant

\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{2-(K+1-2k)\%n_{i}-(K+1-2\ell)\%n_{i}}\frac{(1-q)^{2}}{\left(1-\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n_{i}}\right)^{2}}

-(\xi\chi^{M_{ii}})^{(k+\ell)(1+K)}(-1)g_{(\xi\chi^{M_{ii}})^{2k-K-1}}(1,T)g_{(\xi\chi^{M_{ii}})^{2\ell-K-1}}(1,T)\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{2}\frac{\left(1-\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{-n_{i}}\right)^{2}}{\left(1-q\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n_{i}}\right)^{2}}

The exponent
(k+\ell)(1+K)
simplifies to
(1+K)^{2}\equiv 1+K\bmod 2
while
g_{(\xi\chi^{M_{ii}})^{2k-K-1}}g_{(\xi\chi^{M_{ii}})^{2\ell-K-1}}
simplifies to
q(\xi\chi^{M_{ii}})^{k-\ell}(-1)
and the characters cancel since
k-\ell\equiv k+\ell\equiv 1+K\bmod 2
. The expressions
K+1-2k
and
K+1-2\ell
are additive inverses mod
n_{i}
and not both zero so taking
\%n_{i}
and summing gives
n_{i}
. This gives the determinant

\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{2-n_{i}}\frac{(1-q)^{2}}{\left(1-\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n_{i}}\right)^{2}}-q\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{2}\frac{\left(1-\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{-n_{i}}\right)^{2}}{\left(1-q\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n_{i}}\right)^{2}}=\frac{\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{2-n_{i}}\left(1-q\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{-n_{i}}\right)}{\left(1-q\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n_{i}}\right).}

Dividing the
k,k
entry
\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{1-(K+1-2k)\%n_{i}}\frac{1-q}{1-q\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n_{i}}}
by that determinant, we obtain the
\ell,\ell
entry of the inverse matrix

\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n_{i}-1-(K+1-2k)\%n_{i}}\frac{1-q}{1-q\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{-n_{i}}}

The substitution
x\mapsto\frac{g^{2}}{q^{2}x}
applied to the
\ell,\ell
entry
\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{1-(K+1-2l)\%n_{i}}\frac{1-q}{1-q\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n_{i}}}
gives

\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{((K+1-2\ell)\%n_{i}-1}\frac{1-q}{1-q\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{-n_{i}}}

and these are equal since
(K+1-2\ell)\%n_{i}=n_{i}-(K+1-2k)\%n_{i}
. An identical argument works for the
k,k
entry.

Dividing minus the
k,\ell
entry
(\xi\chi^{M_{ii}})^{\ell(1+K)}(-1)g_{(\xi\chi^{M_{ii}})^{2k-K-1}}\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)\frac{1-\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{-n_{i}}}{1-q\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n_{i}}}
by the determinant gives the
k,\ell
entry of the inverse matrix

-(\xi\chi^{M_{ii}})^{\ell(1+K)}(-1)g_{(\xi\chi^{M_{ii}})^{2k-K-1}}\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{-1+n_{i}}\frac{1-\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{-n_{i}}}{1-q\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{-n_{i}}}

=(\xi\chi^{M_{ii}})^{\ell(1+K)}(-1)g_{(\xi\chi^{M_{ii}})^{2k-K-1}}\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{-1}\frac{1-\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n_{i}}}{1-q\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{-n_{i}}}

while the substitution
x\mapsto\frac{g^{2}}{q^{2}x}
applied to the
k,\ell
entry gives

(\xi\chi^{M_{ii}})^{\ell(1+K)}(-1)g_{(\xi\chi^{M_{ii}})^{2k-K-1}}\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{-1}\frac{1-\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n_{i}}}{1-q\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{-n_{i}}}

and these are equal. An identical argument works for the
\ell,k
entry. ∎

The local scattering matrix
(\Gamma_{\pi,k,\ell}(x,K))_{k,\ell}
has the same property.

We will need some further notation to separate out the contribution of one prime
\pi
to the series
D(x,\vec{f},k)
. The part of
D
coprime to
\pi
, with a twist by the character
\left(\frac{\pi^{\ell}}{*}\right)_{\xi\chi^{M_{rr}}}
for
\ell\bmod n_{r}
, is

D^{(\pi)}(x,\vec{f},k,\pi^{\ell})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}f_{r}\in\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]^{+}\\
\deg f_{r}\equiv k\bmod n\\
\pi\nmid f_{r}\end{subarray}}a(\vec{f},f_{r})\left(\frac{\pi^{\ell}}{f_{r}}\right)_{\xi\chi^{M_{rr}}}x^{\deg f_{r}}.

(3.11)

The following lemma gives a formula to combine the
\pi
-parts and
\pi
-coprime parts of the series
D
.

Lemma 3.4
.

For any prime
\pi
, we have the following equality of vectors:

\begin{split}&\varepsilon_{\pi}\left((\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{(k-\ell\deg\pi)(\ell\deg\pi)}(-1)D^{(\pi)}(x,\vec{f}^{(\pi)},k-\ell\deg\pi,\pi^{2\ell}F_{\pi}^{-1})\right)_{k,\ell}\\
&\cdot\left(\left(\frac{F^{(\pi)}}{\pi^{\ell}}\right)_{\xi\chi^{M_{rr}}}^{-1}D_{\pi}(x,\vec{f}_{\pi},\ell)\right)_{\ell}\\
&=\left(D(x,\vec{f},k)\right)_{k}\end{split}

(3.12)

where
\varepsilon_{\pi}
is the
n
th root of unity defined in equation (
3.4
).

Proof.

By twisted multiplicativity and the character identity
(\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{-n_{ri}}=\chi^{M_{ri}}
,

\begin{split}a(f_{1},\ldots f_{r-1},f_{r})=&\varepsilon_{\pi}\left(\frac{f_{r}^{(\pi)}}{(f_{r})_{\pi}}\right)_{\chi}^{M_{rr}}\left(\frac{(f_{r})_{\pi}}{f_{r}^{(\pi)}}\right)_{\chi}^{M_{rr}}\\
&\left(\frac{F_{\pi}}{f_{r}^{(\pi)}}\right)_{\xi\chi^{M_{rr}}}^{-1}a(f_{1}^{(\pi)},\ldots f_{r}^{(\pi)})\\
&\left(\frac{F^{(\pi)}}{(f_{r})_{\pi}}\right)_{\xi\chi^{M_{rr}}}^{-1}a((f_{1})_{\pi},\ldots(f_{r})_{\pi}).\end{split}

(3.13)

Note that
\varepsilon_{\pi}
is independent of
f_{r}
, the second line only depends on
f_{r}^{(\pi)}
, and the third line only depends on
(f_{r})_{\pi}
. Using quadratic reciprocity and the
q\equiv 1\bmod 4
assumption, the residues in the first line can be simplified to
(\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{(\deg f_{r}^{(\pi)})(\deg(f_{r})_{\pi})}(-1)\left(\frac{(f_{r})_{\pi}}{f_{r}^{(\pi)}}\right)^{2}_{\xi\chi^{M_{rr}}}
. This depends on both
(f_{r})_{\pi}
and
f_{r}^{(\pi)}
, but really only on
v_{\pi}(f_{r})\bmod n_{r}
,
\left(\frac{\pi}{f_{r}^{(\pi)}}\right)_{\xi\chi^{M_{rr}}}
, and
\deg f_{r}^{(\pi)}\bmod n_{r}
.

Therefore

\begin{split}&\varepsilon_{\pi}\sum_{\ell=0}^{n-1}\,(\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{(k-\ell\deg\pi)(\ell\deg\pi)}(-1)D^{(\pi)}(x,\vec{f}^{(\pi)},k-\ell\deg\pi,\pi^{2\ell}F_{\pi}^{-1})\left(\frac{F^{(\pi)}}{\pi^{\ell}}\right)_{\xi\chi^{M_{rr}}}^{-1}D_{\pi}(x,\vec{f}_{\pi},\ell)\\
&=\sum_{\ell=0}^{n-1}\,\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}f_{r}^{(\pi)}\in\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]^{+}\\
\deg f_{r}^{(\pi)}\equiv k-\ell\deg\pi\bmod n_{r}\\
\pi\nmid f_{r}^{(\pi)}\end{subarray}}\,\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(f_{r})_{\pi}=\pi^{d}\\
d\geq 0\\
d\equiv\ell\bmod n_{r}\end{subarray}}\,\varepsilon_{\pi}(\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{(k-\ell\deg\pi)(\ell\deg\pi)}(-1)\left(\frac{\pi^{2\ell}}{f_{r}^{(\pi)}}\right)_{\xi\chi^{M_{rr}}}\\
&\qquad\left(\frac{F^{(\pi)}}{\pi^{\ell}}\right)_{\xi\chi^{M_{rr}}}^{-1}\left(\frac{F_{\pi}}{f_{r}^{(\pi)}}\right)_{\xi\chi^{M_{rr}}}^{-1}a(\vec{f}^{(\pi)},f_{r}^{(\pi)})a(\vec{f}_{\pi},(f_{r})_{\pi})x^{\deg f_{r}^{(\pi)}+\deg(f_{r})_{\pi}}\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}f_{r}\in\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]^{+}\\
\deg f_{r}\equiv k\bmod n_{r}\end{subarray}}a(\vec{f},f_{r})x^{\deg f_{r}}=D(x,\vec{f},k).\end{split}

This proves formula (
3.12
).
∎

Note that the vector
\left(\left(\frac{F^{(\pi)}}{\pi^{\ell}}\right)_{\xi\chi^{M_{rr}}}^{-1}D_{\pi}(x,\vec{f}_{\pi},\ell)\right)_{\ell}
satisfies the same functional equation (
3.10
) as
\left(D_{\pi}(x,\vec{f},\ell)\right)_{\ell}
, because of equation (
3.5
).

We will need a matrix identity which generalizes Lemma
3.4
, using a collection of axiomatic Dirichlet series constructed from different matrices. Fix
m\in\mathbb{Z}
; let
M_{00}=\cdots=M_{0\,(r-1)}=0
, and
M_{0r}=m(M_{rr}+n/2)\%n
. Let
M_{m(M_{rr}+n/2)}
be the
r+1\times r+1
symmetric matrix constructed by augmenting
M
with a zeroth row and column, with entries
M_{0j}
. The
n_{r0}
defined in equation (
2.8
) for the matrix
M_{m(M_{rr}+n/2)}
will be congruent to
-m
modulo
n_{r}
. We define

\displaystyle D(x,(f_{0},\vec{f}),k;M_{m(M_{rr}+n/2)})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}f_{r}\in\mathbb{F}_{q}[T]^{+}\\
\deg f_{r}\equiv k\bmod n\end{subarray}}a(f_{0},\vec{f},f_{r};M_{m(M_{rr}+n/2)})x^{\deg f_{r}}

\displaystyle D_{\pi}(x,(f_{0},\vec{f}),k;M_{m(M_{rr}+n/2)})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\geq 0\\
j\equiv k\bmod n\end{subarray}}a(f_{0},\vec{f},\pi^{j};M_{m(M_{rr}+n/2)})x^{j\deg\pi}

\displaystyle D^{(\pi)}(x,(f_{0},\vec{f}),k,\pi^{\ell};M_{m(M_{rr}+n/2)})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}f_{r}\in\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]^{+}\\
\deg f_{r}\equiv k\bmod n\\
\pi\nmid f_{r}\end{subarray}}a(f_{0},\vec{f},f_{r};M_{m(M_{rr}+n/2)})\left(\frac{\pi^{\ell}}{f_{r}}\right)_{\xi\chi^{M_{rr}}}x^{\deg f_{r}}

where the coefficients
a(f_{0},\vec{f},f_{r};M_{m(M_{rr}+n/2)})
are constructed axiomatically from the matrix
M_{m(M_{rr}+n/2)}
. This notation makes the dependence on the underlying matrix explicit. We will continue to omit writing down this matrix when it is the original fixed matrix
M
.

Lemma 3.5
.

For any prime
\pi
and
v\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have the following equality of
n_{r}\times n_{r}
matrices:

\begin{split}&\varepsilon_{\pi}\left((\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{(k-\ell\deg\pi)\ell\deg\pi}(-1)D^{(\pi)}(x,\vec{f}^{(\pi)},k-\ell\deg\pi,\pi^{2\ell+v}F_{\pi}^{-1})\right)_{k,\ell}\\
&\cdot\left((\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{\ell m\deg\pi}(-1)\left(\frac{F^{(\pi)}}{\pi^{\ell-m}}\right)_{\xi\chi^{M_{rr}}}^{-1}D_{\pi}(x,\pi,\vec{f}_{\pi},\ell-m;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})\right)_{\ell,m}\\
&=\left((\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{km\deg\pi}(-1)D(x,\pi,\vec{f},k-m\deg\pi;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})\right)_{k,m}.\end{split}

(3.14)

Proof.

Lemma
3.4
for the matrix
M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)}
gives:

\begin{split}&\varepsilon_{\pi}\left((\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{(k-\ell\deg\pi)\ell\deg\pi}(-1)D^{(\pi)}(x,1,\vec{f}^{(\pi)},k-\ell\deg\pi,\pi^{2\ell+v+2m}F_{\pi}^{-1};M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})\right)_{k,\ell}\\
&\cdot\left(\left(\frac{F^{(\pi)}}{\pi^{\ell}}\right)_{\xi\chi^{M_{rr}}}^{-1}D_{\pi}(x,\pi,\vec{f}_{\pi},\ell;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})\right)_{\ell}\\
&=\left(D(x,\pi,\vec{f},k;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})\right)_{k}\end{split}

with the same
\varepsilon_{\pi}
used for
M
. Simplifying
D^{(\pi)}(x,1,\vec{f}^{(\pi)},k-\ell\deg\pi,\pi^{2\ell+v+2m}F_{\pi}^{-1};M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})
to
D^{(\pi)}(x,\vec{f}^{(\pi)},k-\ell\deg\pi,\pi^{2\ell+v+2m}F_{\pi}^{-1})
, making the substitutions
k\mapsto k-m\deg\pi
and
\ell\mapsto\ell-m
, and rearranging powers of
(\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})(-1)
, we have:

\begin{split}&\varepsilon_{\pi}\left((\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{(k-\ell\deg\pi)\ell\deg\pi}(-1)D^{(\pi)}(x,\vec{f}^{(\pi)},k-\ell\deg\pi,\pi^{2\ell+v}F_{\pi}^{-1})\right)_{k,\ell}\\
&\cdot\left((\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{\ell m\deg\pi}(-1)\left(\frac{F^{(\pi)}}{\pi^{\ell-m}}\right)_{\xi\chi^{M_{rr}}}^{-1}D_{\pi}(x,\pi,\vec{f}_{\pi},\ell-m;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})\right)_{\ell}\\
&=\left((\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{km\deg\pi}(-1)D(x,\pi,\vec{f},k-m\deg\pi;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})\right)_{k}.\end{split}

The first matrix is independent of
m
. Extending the latter two vectors to matrices indexed by
m
gives the result.
∎

The simplest case of Lemma
3.5
is for
\vec{f}=\vec{1}
and
\deg\pi=1
:

\begin{split}&\left((\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{(k-\ell)\ell}(-1)D^{(\pi)}(x,\vec{1},k-\ell,\pi^{2\ell+v})\right)_{k,\ell}\\
&\cdot\left((\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{\ell m}(-1)D_{\pi}(x,\pi,\vec{1},\ell-m;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})\right)_{\ell,m}\\
&=\left((\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{km}(-1)D(x,\pi,\vec{1},k-m;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})\right)_{k,m}.\end{split}

(3.15)

These three matrices can be computed explicitly. The second matrix is evaluated using the formulas for local generating series (
3.6
), (
3.7
). For
m=\ell
,

D_{\pi}(x,\pi,\vec{1},\ell-m;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})=\left(1-q^{-1}\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n_{r}}\right)^{-1}.

For
m\equiv 1-v-\ell\bmod n_{r}
,

\begin{split}&D_{\pi}(x,\pi,\vec{1},\ell-m;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})\\
&=\left(1-q^{-1}\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n_{r}}\right)^{-1}\left(\frac{g_{(\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{1-v-2m}}(1,\pi)}{q}\right)\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{(1-v-2m)\%n_{r}}.\end{split}

And for
m\not\equiv\ell,1-v-\ell\bmod n_{r}
,
D_{\pi}(x,\pi,\vec{1},\ell-m;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})=0
. In addition, there are special entries in the matrix when
m\equiv\ell\equiv 1-v-\ell\bmod n_{r}
. In this case
D_{\pi}(x,\pi,\vec{1},\ell-m;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})=1
.

The matrix
\left((\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{\ell m}(-1)D_{\pi}(x,\pi,\vec{1},\ell-m;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})\right)_{\ell,m}
is conjugate under a permutation matrix to a block diagonal matrix of
2\times 2
blocks, where each nontrivial
2\times 2
block has determinant equal to
(\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{1-v}(-1)\left(1-q^{-1}\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n_{r}}\right)^{-1}
. Therefore the inverse matrix has entries which are monomials in
x
, with no poles.

The third matrix is related to the second one by Axiom
4
. Note that all
\pi
of degree
1
are related by a linear change of variables
T\mapsto T-c
. If a set of coefficients
a(f_{1}(T),\ldots f_{n}(T))
satisfy the five axioms, then so do
a(f_{1}(T-c),\ldots f_{n}(T-c))
. By the uniqueness part of
[
45
, Theorem 1.1]
, the coefficients are invariant under
T\mapsto T-c
. Therefore the matrix
\left((\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{km}(-1)D(x,\pi,\vec{1},k-m;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})\right)_{k,m}
is the same for all
\pi
of degree
1
. Summing these matrices over all
\pi
of degree
1
is the same as multiplying by
q
. Thus, by Axiom
4
, if we replace
q
with
1/q
, replace each
q
-Weil number
\alpha
with
1/\bar{\alpha}
, and replace
x
with
qx
, we transform back and forth between
\left((\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{km}(-1)D(x,\pi,\vec{1},k-m;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})\right)_{k,m}
and
\left((\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{\ell m}(-1)D_{\pi}(x,\pi,\vec{1},\ell-m;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})\right)_{\ell,m}
.

This means that, for
m\equiv k\bmod n_{r}

D(x,\pi,\vec{1},k-m;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})=\left(1-q\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n_{r}}\right)^{-1}.

For
m\equiv 1-v-k\bmod n_{r}
,

\begin{split}&D(x,\pi,\vec{1},k-m;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})\\
&=\left(1-q\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n_{r}}\right)^{-1}g_{(\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{1-v-2m}}(1,\pi)\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{(1-v-2m)\%n_{r}}.\end{split}

And for
m\not\equiv k,1-v-k\bmod n_{r}
,
D(x,\pi,\vec{1},\ell-m;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})=0
. In addition, there are special entries in the matrix when
m\equiv k\equiv 1-v-k\bmod n_{r}
. In this case,
D(x,\pi,\vec{1},k-m;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})=1
.

This matrix is also conjugate to a block-diagonal matrix of
2\times 2
blocks, where each nontrivial
2\times 2
block has determinant
(\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{1-v}(-1)\left(1-q\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n_{r}}\right)^{-1}
. The inverse matrix has entries which are monomials in
x
, with no poles.

The first matrix
\left((\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{(k-\ell)\ell}(-1)D^{(\pi)}(x,\vec{1},k-\ell,\pi^{2\ell+v})\right)_{k,\ell}
can be computed from the other two. It has the interesting property of being mapped to its inverse by the change of variables
q\mapsto 1/q

\alpha\mapsto 1/\bar{\alpha}
,
x\mapsto qx
.

We now give functional equations generalizing (
3.8
) and (
3.10
) for the matrices appearing in Lemma
3.5
.

Lemma 3.6
.

For
\pi
prime, suppose that
\pi f_{1}\cdots f_{r-1}
is squarefree, or more generally that the functional equations (
3.8
) hold for
\left(D(x,\pi,\vec{f},k;M_{m(M_{rr}+n/2)}\right)_{k}
for all
m
. Then for
v\in\mathbb{Z}
,

\begin{split}&\left((\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{km\deg\pi}(-1)D(x,\pi,\vec{f},k-m\deg\pi;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})\right)_{k,m}\\
&=\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{\deg F}\left(\Gamma_{k,\ell}(x,\deg F-v\deg\pi)\right)_{k,\ell}\\
&\phantom{=}\cdot\left((\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{\ell m\deg\pi}(-1)D\left(\frac{g^{2}}{q^{2}x},\pi,\vec{f},\ell-m\deg\pi;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)}\right)\right)_{\ell,m}\\
&\phantom{=}\cdot\left(\Delta_{m,m}(x,v)^{\deg\pi}\right)_{m,m}\end{split}

(3.16)

where
\left(\Gamma_{k,\ell}(x,\deg F-v\deg\pi)\right)_{k,\ell}
is the
n_{r}\times n_{r}
scattering matrix defined in Proposition
3.1
, and
\left(\Delta_{m,m}(x,v)^{\deg\pi}\right)_{m,m}
is a diagonal matrix with entries
\Delta_{m,m}(x,v)^{\deg\pi}=\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{((-v-2m)\%n_{r})\deg\pi}
.

Note that the
m=0
column of the matrix identity (
3.16
) recovers the functional equation (
3.8
) for
\left(D(x,\pi,\vec{f},k;M_{v(M_{rr}+n/2)}\right)_{k}
.

Proof.

Equation
3.9
for the matrix
M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)}
gives:

\begin{split}&D(x,\pi,\vec{f},k-m\deg\pi;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})\\
&=\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{\deg F+((-v-2m)\%n_{r})\deg\pi+1-(\deg F-v\deg\pi+1-2k)\%n_{r}}\frac{1-q}{1-q\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n_{r}}}\\
&\cdot D\left(\frac{g^{2}}{q^{2}x},\pi,\vec{f},k-m\deg\pi;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)}\right)\\
&+(\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{(\ell-m\deg\pi)(1+\deg F-v\deg\pi)}(-1)g_{(\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{2k-\deg F+v\deg\pi-1}}\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{\deg F+((-v-2m)\%n_{r})\deg\pi+1}\\
&\cdot\frac{1-\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{-n_{r}}}{1-q\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n_{r}}}D\left(\frac{g^{2}}{q^{2}x},\pi,\vec{f},\ell-m\deg\pi;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)}\right)\end{split}

where
\ell=1+\deg F-v\deg\pi-k
. Multiplying through by
(\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{km\deg\pi}(-1)
, and rewriting these functional equations in matrix form gives the desired statement.
∎

Lemma 3.7
.

Fix
\pi
prime, and suppose that
\pi\nmid f_{1}\cdots f_{r-1}
, or more generally that the functional equations (
3.10
) hold for
\left(\left(\frac{F^{(\pi)}}{\pi^{k}}\right)^{-1}D_{\pi}^{*}(x,\pi,\vec{f}_{\pi},k;M_{m(M_{rr}+n/2)})\right)_{k}
for all
m
. Then for
v\in\mathbb{Z}
,

\begin{split}&\left((\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{km\deg\pi}(-1)\left(\frac{F^{(\pi)}}{\pi^{k-m}}\right)_{\xi\chi^{M_{rr}}}^{-1}D_{\pi}(x,\pi,\vec{f}_{\pi},k-m;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})\right)_{k,m}\\
&=\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{v_{\pi}(F)\deg\pi}\left(\left(\frac{F^{(\pi)}}{\pi}\right)_{\xi\chi^{M-{rr}}}^{k+\ell}\Gamma_{\pi,k,\ell}(x,v_{\pi}(F)-v)\right)_{k,\ell}\\
&\phantom{=}\cdot\left((\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{\ell m\deg\pi}(-1)\left(\frac{F^{(\pi)}}{\pi^{\ell-m}}\right)_{\xi\chi^{M_{rr}}}^{-1}D_{\pi}\left(\frac{g^{2}}{q^{2}x},\pi,\vec{f}_{\pi},\ell-m;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)}\right)\right)_{\ell,m}\\
&\phantom{=}\cdot\left(\Delta_{m,m}(x,v)^{\deg\pi}\right)_{m,m}\end{split}

(3.17)

where
\left(\left(\frac{F^{(\pi)}}{\pi}\right)_{\xi\chi^{M-{rr}}}^{k+\ell}\Gamma_{\pi,k,\ell}(x,v_{\pi}(F)-v\right)_{k,\ell}
is the
n_{r}\times n_{r}
scattering matrix defined in Proposition
3.10
, as applied to
M_{v(M_{rr}+n/2)}
, and
\left(\Delta_{m,m}(x,v)^{\deg\pi}\right)_{m,m}
is as defined in Lemma
3.6
.

This lemma follows from Proposition
3.2
in the same way that Lemma
3.6
follows from Proposition
3.1
.

Our goal is to prove that the same functional equations hold for all
\vec{f}
, whether or not
f_{1}\cdots f_{r-1}
is squarefree. We first prove a technical statement showing the compatibility of local and global functional equations.

Lemma 3.8
.

Fix
\vec{f}
,
\pi
prime,
v\equiv-v_{\pi}(F)\bmod n_{r}
. Suppose
D(x,\pi,\vec{f}^{(\pi)},k-m\deg\pi;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})
is a rational function of
x
with denominator dividing
1-q\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n_{r}}
for all
k,m
, and that the global matrix functional equation (
3.16
) holds for
\left((\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{km\deg\pi}(-1)D(x,\pi,\vec{f}^{(\pi)},k-m\deg\pi;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})\right)_{k,m}
. Suppose also that
D_{\pi}(x,\vec{f}_{\pi},k)
is a rational function of
x
with denominator dividing
1-q^{-\deg\pi}\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n_{r}\deg\pi}
for all
k
, and that the local vector functional equation (
3.10
) holds for
\left(D_{\pi}(x,\vec{f}_{\pi},k)\right)_{k}
. Then
D(x,\vec{f},k)
is a rational function of
x
with denominator dividing
1-q\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n_{r}}
for all
k
, and the global vector functional equation (
3.8
) holds for
\left(D(x,\vec{f},k)\right)_{k}
.

Proof.

Lemma
3.5
applied to
\vec{f}^{(\pi)}
gives

\begin{split}&\left((\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{(k-\ell\deg\pi)\ell\deg\pi}(-1)D^{(\pi)}(x,\vec{f}^{(\pi)},k-\ell\deg\pi,\pi^{2\ell+v})\right)_{k,\ell}\\
&\cdot\left((\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{\ell m\deg\pi}(-1)\left(\frac{F^{(\pi)}}{\pi^{\ell-m}}\right)_{\xi\chi^{M_{rr}}}^{-1}D_{\pi}(x,\pi,\vec{1},\ell-m;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})\right)_{\ell,m}\\
&=\left((\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{km\deg\pi}(-1)D(x,\pi,\vec{f}^{(\pi)},k-m\deg\pi;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})\right)_{k,m}.\end{split}

The second matrix is computed explicitly in the discussion following Lemma
3.5
, for
F^{(\pi)}=1
and
\deg\pi=1
. The computation can be extended to all
\pi
and
F^{(\pi)}
using Axiom
3
. The result is that this matrix is conjugate to a block-diagonal matrix of
2\times 2
blocks, where each nontrivial
2\times 2
block has determinant equal to
(\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{(1-v)\deg\pi}(-1)\left(1-q^{-\deg\pi}\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n_{r}\deg\pi}\right)
. Its inverse is a matrix of monomials in
x
.

Lemma
3.4
applied to
\vec{f}
gives

\begin{split}&\varepsilon_{\pi}\left((\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{(k-\ell\deg\pi)(\ell\deg\pi)}(-1)D^{(\pi)}(x,\vec{f}^{(\pi)},k-\ell\deg\pi,\pi^{2\ell-v_{\pi}(F)})\right)_{k,\ell}\\
&\cdot\left(\left(\frac{F^{(\pi)}}{\pi^{\ell}}\right)_{\xi\chi^{M_{rr}}}^{-1}D_{\pi}(x,\vec{f}_{\pi},\ell)\right)_{\ell}\\
&=\left(D(x,\vec{f},k)\right)_{k}.\end{split}

We can combine the two equations:

\begin{split}&\left(D(x,\vec{f},k)\right)_{k}\\
&=\varepsilon_{\pi}\left((\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{km\deg\pi}(-1)D(x,\pi,\vec{f}^{(\pi)},k-m\deg\pi;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})\right)_{k,m}\\
&\cdot\left((\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{\ell m\deg\pi}(-1)\left(\frac{F^{(\pi)}}{\pi^{\ell-m}}\right)_{\xi\chi^{M_{rr}}}^{-1}D_{\pi}(x,\pi,\vec{1},\ell-m;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})\right)_{\ell,m}^{-1}\\
&\cdot\left(\left(\frac{F^{(\pi)}}{\pi^{\ell}}\right)_{\xi\chi^{M_{rr}}}^{-1}D_{\pi}(x,\vec{f}_{\pi},\ell)\right)_{\ell}.\end{split}

(3.18)

By hypothesis, the vector in line four of (
3.18
)
satisfies a functional equation with scattering matrix
\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{v_{\pi}(F)\deg\pi}\left(\left(\frac{F^{(\pi)}}{\pi}\right)_{\xi\chi^{M-{rr}}}^{k+\ell}\Gamma_{\pi,k,\ell}(x,v_{\pi}(F))\right)_{k,\ell}
. By Lemma
3.7
, the matrix with inverse in line three of (
3.18
)
satisfies a functional equation with scattering matrix
\left(\left(\frac{F^{(\pi)}}{\pi}\right)_{\xi\chi^{M-{rr}}}^{k+\ell}\Gamma_{\pi,k,\ell}(x,-v)\right)_{k,\ell}
and a diagonal matrix
\left(\Delta_{m,m}(x,v)^{\deg\pi}\right)_{m,m}
on the right. Since
v\equiv-v_{\pi}(F)\bmod n
, we have
\Gamma_{\pi,k,\ell}(x,v_{\pi}(F))=\Gamma_{\pi,k,\ell}(x,-v)
. Thus the product of lines three and four satisfies a functional equation in
x\mapsto\frac{g^{2}}{q^{2}x}
, with scattering matrix
\left(\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{v_{\pi}(F)\deg\pi}\Delta_{m,m}(x,v)^{-\deg\pi}\right)_{m,m}
. This scattering matrix is a diagonal matrix of monomials in
x
.

The product of lines three and four of (
3.18
) is a priori a vector of rational functions with denominators dividing
1-q^{-\deg\pi}\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n_{r}\deg\pi}
. However, if any of these functions had any poles at
\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n_{r}}=q
, the functional equation would transform them to poles at
\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n_{r}}=q^{-1}
, which cannot exist. Therefore the product must in fact be a vector of polynomials. Then from our hypothesis on the rationality of
D(x,\pi,\vec{f}^{(\pi)},k-m\deg\pi;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})
, we have that
\left(D(x,\vec{f},k)\right)_{k}
is a vector of rational functions with denominators dividing
1-q\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n_{r}}
.

By hypothesis,
\left((\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{km\deg\pi}(-1)D(x,\pi,\vec{f}^{(\pi)},k-m\deg\pi;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})\right)_{k,m}
satisfies a functional equation in
x\mapsto\frac{g^{2}}{q^{2}x}
, with scattering matrix
\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{\deg F^{(\pi)}}\left(\Gamma_{k,\ell}(x,\deg F^{(\pi)}-v\deg\pi)\right)_{k,\ell}
and a diagonal matrix
\left(\Delta_{m,m}(x,v)^{\deg\pi}\right)_{m,m}
on the right. So all together,
\left(D(x,\vec{f},k)\right)_{k}
satisfies a functional equation in
x\mapsto\frac{g^{2}}{q^{2}x}
, with scattering matrix

\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{\deg F^{(\pi)}+v_{\pi}(F)\deg\pi}\left(\Gamma_{k,\ell}(x,\deg F^{(\pi)}-v\deg\pi)\right)_{k,\ell}=\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{\deg F}\left(\Gamma_{k,\ell}(x,\deg F)\right)_{k,\ell}

as desired.
∎

Local generating series
D_{\pi}(x,\vec{f}_{\pi},k)
satisfying the functional equation (
3.10
) have been constructed in many cases by combinatorial means. Then Lemma
3.8
can be used to show that the global series assembled from these local ones by twisted multiplicativity satisfy the functional equation (
3.8
). The method of proof is an induction of the number of square factors of
f_{1}\cdots f_{r-1}
. This type of proof is carried out in
[
12
]
over number fields and in
[
27
]
over the rational function field. It allows global Weyl group multiple Dirichlet series to be constructed from their local parts. The axiomatic approach does not require a separate combinatorial construction of the local parts, and it verifies the local and global functional equations simultaneously. Our main theorem is proven by a different type of induction.

Let
D(x)
be a power series in
x
, whose coefficients are linear combinations of
q
-Weil numbers. We say that
D(x)
is “sharp” if each coefficient of
x^{j}
in
D(x)
is a linear combination of
q
-Weil numbers of weights greater than
j
, and “flat” if each coefficient of
x^{j}
in
D(x)
is a linear combination of
q
-Weil numbers of weights less than
j
. Sharp series and flat series are closed under linear combinations, products, and inverses.

By Axiom
5
, for
d_{1}+\cdots+d_{r-1}\geq 2
, any series of the form

q^{\frac{-\sum d_{i}-1}{2}}\sum_{\deg f_{1}=d_{1},\ldots\deg f_{r-1}=d_{r-1}}D(x,\vec{f},k)

is sharp, while any series of the form

q^{\frac{-\sum d_{i}+1}{2}}D_{\pi}(x,(\pi^{d_{1}},\ldots\pi^{d_{r-1}}),k)

is flat.

Theorem 3.9
.

For all
\vec{f}=(f_{1},\ldots f_{r-1})\in(\mathbb{F}_{q}[T]^{+})^{r-1}
,
D(x,\vec{f},k)
is a rational function of
x
with denominator dividing
1-q\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n_{r}}
and the global functional equation (
3.8
) is satisfied.
D_{\pi}(x,\vec{f},k)
is a rational function of
x
with denominator dividing
1-q^{-\deg\pi}\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n_{r}\deg\pi}
and the local functional equation (
3.10
) is satisfied.

Proof.

The proof is by induction on
\deg f_{1}+\cdots+\deg f_{r-1}
and
v_{\pi}f_{1}+\cdots+v_{\pi}f_{r-1}
. Note that the matrix
M
is not fixed in the argument, though we always assume
\gcd(n,M_{rr}+n/2)|M_{ri}
, for all
i
. At each step in the induction, the global functional equation is proven for all matrices
M
and all
\vec{f}
with
\deg f_{1}+\cdots+\deg f_{r-1}
fixed, and the local functional equation is proven for all matrices
M
, all
\pi
, and all
\vec{f}
with
v_{\pi}f_{1}+\cdots+v_{\pi}f_{r-1}
fixed.

If
\deg f_{1}+\cdots+\deg f_{r-1}=0
or
1
, then the rationality property and global functional equation hold by Proposition
3.1
. If
v_{\pi}f_{1}+\cdots+v_{\pi}f_{r-1}=0
or
1
, then the rationality property and local functional equation hold by Proposition
3.2
.

For the inductive step, suppose that the global rationality property and functional equation are known for all
M
and all
\vec{f}
with
\deg f_{1}+\cdots+\deg f_{r-1}<A
, and the local rationality property and functional equation are known for all
M
and all
\vec{f}
with
v_{\pi}f_{1}+\cdots+v_{\pi}f_{r-1}<A
. If
\vec{f}=(f_{1},\ldots f_{r-1})
has
\deg f_{1}+\cdots+\deg f_{r-1}=A
and
f_{1}\cdots f_{r-1}
is squarefree, then the global rationality property and functional equation are known for
\vec{f}
by Proposition
3.8
. If
\pi^{2}|f_{1}\cdots f_{r-1}
for some prime
\pi
, we apply Lemma
3.8
. The vector
(\pi,\vec{f}^{(\pi)})
has
\deg\pi+\deg f_{1}^{(\pi)}+\cdots+f_{r-1}^{(\pi)}<A
, so by induction, the global rationality property and functional equation hold for each extended matrix
M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)}
with
v\equiv-v_{\pi}(F)
and
m=0,\ldots n-1
. Moreover, unless
\pi
is linear and each
f_{i}
is a power of
\pi
, we have
v_{\pi}f_{1}+\cdots+v_{\pi}f_{r-1}<A
, so the local rationality property and functional equation hold for
\vec{f}
at
\pi
. Therefore by Lemma
3.8
, the global rationality property and functional equation hold for
\vec{f}
. This establishes the global rationality property and functional equation for all
\vec{f}
except when the
f_{i}
are all powers of the same linear polynomial.

We now consider the sum

\sum_{\deg f_{1}=d_{1},\ldots\deg f_{r-1}=d_{r-1}}\left(D(x,\vec{f},k)\right)_{k}-\sum_{\deg\pi=1}\left(D(x,(\pi^{d_{1}},\ldots\pi^{d_{r-1}}),k)\right)_{k}

(3.19)

which satisfies the global rationality property and functional equation (
3.8
). Note that, by Axiom
3
, the second sum is
q\left(D(x,(\pi^{d_{1}},\ldots\pi^{d_{r-1}}),k)\right)_{k}
for an arbitrary degree 1 monic polynomial
\pi
.

We use Lemma
3.4
to write the second sum as

\begin{split}&q\left(D(x,(\pi^{d_{1}},\ldots\pi^{d_{r-1}}),k)\right)_{k}\\
&=q\left((\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{(k-\ell)\ell}(-1)D^{(\pi)}(x,\vec{1},k-\ell,\pi^{2\ell+v})\right)_{k,\ell}\left(D_{\pi}(x,(\pi^{d_{1}},\ldots\pi^{d_{r-1}}),\ell)\right)_{\ell}\end{split}

where
v\equiv-d_{1}n_{r1}-\cdots-d_{r-1}n_{r\,(r-1)}\bmod n_{r}
. Further, by equation (
3.15
),

\begin{split}&q\left((\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{(k-\ell)\ell}(-1)D^{(\pi)}(x,\vec{1},k-\ell,\pi^{2\ell+v})\right)_{k,\ell}\\
&=\left((\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{km}(-1)D(x,\pi,\vec{1},k-m;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})\right)_{k,m}\\
&\cdot\left((\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{\ell m}(-1)D_{\pi}(x,\pi,\vec{1},\ell-m;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})\right)_{\ell,m}^{-1}\end{split}

and so we may multiply (
3.19
) by
q^{\frac{-A-1}{2}}\left((\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{km}(-1)D(x,\pi,\vec{1},k-m;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})\right)_{k,m}^{-1}
to obtain

\begin{split}&\left((\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{km}(-1)D(x,\pi,\vec{1},k-m;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})\right)_{k,m}^{-1}\left(q^{\frac{-A-1}{2}}\sum_{\deg f_{i}=d_{i}}D(x,\vec{f},k)\right)_{k}\\
&-\left((\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{km}(-1)D_{\pi}(x,\pi,\vec{1},k-m;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})\right)_{k,m}^{-1}\left(q^{\frac{-A+1}{2}}D_{\pi}(x,(\pi^{d_{1}},\ldots\pi^{d_{r-1}}),k)\right)_{k}.\end{split}

(3.20)

By the discussion following Lemma
3.4
,
q^{\frac{-A-1}{2}}\left((\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{km}(-1)D(x,\pi,\vec{1},k-m;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})\right)_{k,m}^{-1}
is a matrix of monomials in
x
. From the rationality property of (
3.19
), we have that (
3.20
) is a vector of rational functions with denominators dividing
1-q\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n_{r}}
.

We know that formula (
3.19
) satisfies a functional equation in
x\mapsto\frac{g^{2}}{q^{2}x}
with scattering matrix
\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{\deg F}\left(\Gamma_{k,\ell}(x,\deg F)\right)_{k,\ell}
. By Lemma
3.6
,
\left((\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{km}(-1)D(x,\pi,\vec{1},k-m;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})\right)_{k,m}
satisfies a functional equation with scattering matrix
\left(\Gamma_{k,\ell}(x,-v)\right)_{k,\ell}
and diagonal matrix
\left(\Delta_{m,m}(x,v)\right)_{m,m}
on the right. Since
v\equiv-\deg F\bmod n
, we have
\Gamma_{k,\ell}(x,\deg F)=\Gamma_{k,\ell}(x,-v)
. Thus, (
3.20
) satisfies a functional equation with scattering matrix
\left(\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{\deg F}\Delta_{m,m}(x,v)^{-1}\right)_{m,m}
. This scattering matrix is a diagonal matrix of monomials in
x
.

If any of the entries of (
3.20
) had any poles at
\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n_{r}}=q^{-1}
, the functional equation would transform them to poles at
\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n_{r}}=q
, which cannot exist. Therefore (
3.20
) must in fact be a vector of polynomials.

By the discussion preceding this theorem, the series
D(x,\pi,\vec{1},k-m;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})
is sharp (except for a constant term of
1
when
k=m
), and
q^{\frac{-A-1}{2}}\sum D(x,\vec{f},k)
is sharp. Furthermore,
D_{\pi}(x,\pi,\vec{1},k-m;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})
is flat (except for a constant term of
1
when
k=m
), and
q^{\frac{-A+1}{2}}D_{\pi}(x,(\pi^{d_{1}},\ldots\pi^{d_{r-1}}),k)
is flat. It follows that the full first summand of (
3.20
) is sharp and the full second summand is flat. Therefore, there is no cancellation between these summands.

We conclude that each summand of (
3.20
) has entries which are polynomials in
x
. After multiplying the first summand by
q^{\frac{A+1}{2}}\left((\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{km}(-1)D(x,\pi,\vec{1},k-m;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})\right)_{k,m}
, we see that each
\sum D(x,\vec{f},k)
is a rational function of
x
with denominator dividing
1-q\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n_{r}}
. After multiplying the second summand by
q^{\frac{A-1}{2}}\left((\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{km}(-1)D_{\pi}(x,\pi,\vec{1},k-m;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})\right)_{k,m}
, we see that each
D_{\pi}(x,(\pi^{d_{1}},\ldots\pi^{d_{r-1}}),k)
is a rational function of
x
with denominator dividing
1-q^{-1}\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n_{r}}
.

Since mapping
x\mapsto\frac{g^{2}}{q^{2}x}
and multiplying by powers of
\frac{qx}{g}
preserve both sharp and flat polynomials, the two summands of (
3.20
) must satisfy the same functional equation individually.

Consider the second summand of (
3.20
). This satisfies a functional equation with scattering matrix
\left(\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{\deg F}\Delta_{m,m}(x,v)^{-1}\right)_{m,m}
.
By Lemma
3.7
,
\left((\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{km}(-1)D_{\pi}(x,\pi,\vec{1},k-m;M_{(v+2m)(M_{rr}+n/2)})\right)_{k,m}
has a functional equation with scattering matrix
\left(\Gamma_{\pi,k,\ell}(x,-v)\right)_{k,\ell}
and diagonal matrix
\left(\Delta_{m,m}(x,v)\right)_{m,m}
on the right. Combining these functional equations, and recalling that
v\equiv-\deg F\bmod n_{r}
, we find that
\left(D_{\pi}(x,(\pi^{d_{1}},\ldots\pi^{d_{r-1}}),k)\right)_{k}
satisfies a functional equation with scattering matrix

\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{\deg F}\left(\Gamma_{\pi,k,\ell}(x,\deg F)\right)_{k,\ell}

as desired.

The local rationality property and functional equation of
\left(D_{\pi}(x,(\pi^{d_{1}},\ldots\pi^{d_{r-1}}),k)\right)_{k}
imply the global rationality property and functional equation of
\left(D(x,(\pi^{d_{1}},\ldots\pi^{d_{r-1}}),k)\right)_{k}
by Lemma
3.8
. This finishes the verification of the global rationality property and functional equations for all
\vec{f}
with
\deg f_{i}=d_{i}
.

For
\pi
of degree
1
, we have already verified the local rationality property and functional equation of
\left(D_{\pi}(x,(\pi^{d_{1}},\ldots\pi^{d_{r-1}}),k)\right)_{k}
. To extend to
\pi
of arbitrary degree, we use Axiom
3
, which expresses the coefficients of
\left(D_{\pi}(x,(\pi^{d_{1}},\ldots\pi^{d_{r-1}}),k)\right)_{k}
as a sum of the
\deg\pi
th powers of the same Weil numbers appearing in the coefficients of
\left(D_{T}(x,(T^{d_{1}},\ldots T^{d_{r-1}}),k)\right)_{k}
, times
\left(\frac{\pi^{\prime}}{\pi}\right)_{\chi}^{\sum_{i=1}^{r}d_{i}M_{ii}}
where
d_{r}
is the coefficient of
x^{r}
. This carries over to the functional equations. Set
d=v_{\pi}(F)=d_{1}n_{r1}+\cdots+d_{r-1}n_{r\,(r-1)}
. The scattering matrix
\left(\Gamma_{\pi,k,\ell}(x,d)\right)_{k,\ell}
is obtained from
\left(\Gamma_{T,k,\ell}(x,d)\right)_{k,\ell}
by raising
x
and each Weil number appearing in each entry to the power
\deg\pi
and multiplying the
k,\ell
entry by
\left(\frac{\pi^{\prime}}{\pi}\right)_{\chi}^{(k-\ell)M_{ii}}
. Examining the formulas in Proposition
3.10
, we see that
\Gamma_{\pi,k,k}(x,d)
is obtained from
\Gamma_{T,k,k}(x,d)
by raising
x
and each Weil number to the power
\deg\pi
while for
k\neq\ell
,
\Gamma_{\pi,k,\ell}(x,d)
is obtained from
\Gamma_{T,k,\ell}(x,d)
by raising
x
and each Weil number to the power
\deg\pi
, except in the
g_{(\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{2k-d-1}}(1,\pi)
term, but this term exactly cancels the
\left(\frac{\pi^{\prime}}{\pi}\right)_{\chi}^{(k-\ell)M_{ii}}
factor since
2k-d-1\equiv k-\ell\bmod n
.

Further, the local rationality property and functional equation extend to
\left(D_{\pi}(x,\vec{f},k)\right)_{k}
with
v_{\pi}(f_{i})=d_{i}
by equation (
3.5
) and by including the necessary residue symbols in the scattering matrix entries. This finishes the verification of the local rationality property and functional equations for all
\vec{f}
with
v_{\pi}f_{i}=d_{i}
.
∎

3.2
Multivariable Functional Equation

The functional equation of Theorem
3.9
applies to single-variable generating series, but this leads to a functional equation for the multivariable series which are our main objects of study.

Let
a(f_{1},\ldots f_{r})
be the axiomatic multiple Dirichlet series coefficients determined by the data of
q
,
\chi
,
M
as before. We continue with the same assumptions on these parameters. Write
\vec{x}=(x_{1},\ldots x_{r})\in\mathbb{C}^{r}
,
\vec{k}=(k_{1},\ldots k_{r})\in\mathbb{Z}^{r}
and let

\displaystyle Z(\vec{x},\vec{k})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}f_{1},\ldots f_{r}\in\mathbb{F}_{q}[T]^{+}\\
\deg f_{i}\equiv k_{i}\bmod n\end{subarray}}a(f_{1},\ldots f_{r})x_{1}^{\deg f_{1}}\cdots x_{r}^{\deg f_{r}}

(3.21)

\displaystyle Z_{\pi}(\vec{x},\vec{k})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1},\ldots j_{r}\geq 0\\
j_{i}\equiv k_{i}\bmod n\end{subarray}}a(\pi^{j_{1}},\ldots\pi^{j_{r}})x_{1}^{j_{1}\deg\pi}\cdots x_{r}^{j_{r}\deg\pi}.

(3.22)

We now introduce notation relevant for the multivariable functional equations. Fix
i\in\{1,\ldots r\}
and assume that
\gcd(n,M_{ii}+n/2)|M_{ij}
, for all
j
. For fixed
k_{1},\ldots k_{i-1},k_{i+1},\ldots k_{r}
, let
K=k_{1}n_{i1}+\cdots+k_{i-1}n_{i\,(i-1)}+k_{i+1}n_{i\,(i+1)}+\cdots+k_{r}n_{ir}
, and let
v_{i}=k_{i+1}M_{i\,(i+1)}+\cdots+k_{r}M_{ir}
.

Define a transformation
\sigma_{i;q,\chi,M}:\mathbb{C}^{r}\to\mathbb{C}^{r}
as follows:

\sigma_{i;q,\chi,M}(\vec{x})_{j}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{g^{2}}{q^{2}x_{i}}&j=i\\
x_{j}\left(\frac{qx_{i}}{g}\right)^{n_{ij}}&j\neq i\end{array}\right..

(3.23)

For any power series
D(x)
, let
S^{k,n}D(x)
be the series consisting of all terms in
D(x)
whose powers of
x
are congruent to
k
mod
n
. This can be computed as
\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{j=0}^{n-1}\zeta^{-jk}D(\zeta^{j}x)
where
\zeta
is a primitive
n
-th root of unity.
S^{k,n}
is a
\mathbb{C}
-linear operation on power series, which satisfies
S^{k,n}x^{K}D(x)=x^{K}S^{k-K,n}D(x)
, and more generally

S^{k,n}(D_{1}D_{2})=\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}(S^{k-j,n}D_{1})(S^{j,n}D_{2}).

If
D(x)
is the power series of a rational function, then so is
S^{k,n}D(x)
.

We need to define an expansion operation that transforms the
n_{r}\times n_{r}
scattering matrices from the previous section into
n\times n
scattering matrices, using the fact that
n_{r}
divides
n
. Let
m
be a natural number dividing
n
. Let
\Gamma(x,K)
be an
m\times m
matrix with entries power series in a variable
x
, depending on an integer
K
, such that the exponent of
x
in each nonzero term in the entry
\Gamma_{k,\ell}(x,K)
is congruent to
k+\ell-K\bmod m
. Then let
E_{m}^{n}\Gamma(x,K)
be the
n\times n
matrix whose entries are given by

(E_{m}^{n}\Gamma)_{k,\ell}(x,K)=S^{k+\ell-K,n}\Gamma_{k\%m,\ell\%m}(x,K).

Theorem 3.10
.

Assume that
q\equiv 1\bmod 4
, and
M
is such that
\gcd(n,M_{ii}+n/2)|M_{ij}
, for all
j
. The vector
(Z(\vec{x},(k_{1},\ldots k_{r}))_{k_{i}}
satisfies a functional equation

(Z(\vec{x},(k_{1},\ldots k_{r})))_{k_{i}}=\left(\chi^{v_{i}(k_{i}+\ell_{i})}(-1)E_{n_{i}}^{n}\Gamma_{k_{i},\ell_{i}}(x_{i},K)\right)_{k_{i},\ell_{i}}(Z(\sigma_{i}(\vec{x}),(k_{1},\ldots k_{i-1},\ell_{i},k_{i+1},\ldots k_{r})))_{\ell_{i}}

(3.24)

where
\left(E_{n_{i}}^{n}\Gamma_{k,\ell}(x,K)\right)_{k,\ell}
is the
n\times n
scattering matrix obtained by applying the expansion operation above to the scattering matrix from Proposition
3.1
, and Theorem
3.9
, namely

\begin{split}E_{n_{i}}^{n}\Gamma_{k,\ell}(x,K)=&q^{(n-n_{i}-(n-n_{i}+1+K-k-\ell)\%n+(1+K-k-\ell)\%n_{i})/n_{i}}\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n-n_{i}+1-(n-n_{i}+1+K-k-\ell)\%n}\\
&\frac{1-q}{1-q^{n/n_{i}}\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n}}\end{split}

when
k\equiv\ell\bmod n_{i}
,

\begin{split}E_{n_{i}}^{n}\Gamma_{k,\ell}(x,K)=&(\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{\ell(1+K)}(-1)g_{(\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{2k-K-1}}\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)\\
&\frac{q^{((k+\ell-K-1)\%n)/n_{i}}\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{(k+\ell-K-1)\%n}-q^{((k+\ell-K-1+n_{i})\%n)/n_{i}}\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{-n_{i}+(k+\ell-K-1+n_{i})\%n}}{1-q^{n/n_{i}}\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n}}\end{split}

for
\ell\equiv 1+K-k\bmod n_{i}
, and all other entries equal to
0
. In addition, there are special entries when
k\equiv\ell\equiv 1+K-k-n_{i}\bmod n
. In this case

\Gamma_{k,\ell}(x,K)=\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{1-n_{i}}.

Proof.

First we will prove the statement for
i=r
. We can write
Z(\vec{x},(k_{1},\ldots k_{r}))
(with
k_{r}
mod
n
) in terms of the
D(x_{r},(f_{1},\ldots f_{r-1}),k_{r})
(with
k_{r}
mod
n_{r}
) from the previous section as follows:

Z(\vec{x},(k_{1},\ldots k_{r}))=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}f_{1},\ldots f_{r-1}\in\mathbb{F}_{q}[T]^{+}\\
\deg f_{i}\equiv k_{i}\bmod n\end{subarray}}x_{1}^{\deg f_{1}}\cdots x_{r-1}^{\deg f_{r-1}}S^{k_{r},n}D(x_{r},(f_{1},\ldots f_{r-1}),k_{r}).

Here we understand
k_{r}
as an integer mod
n
. After applying the functional equation (
3.9
), we obtain

\begin{split}&Z(\vec{x},(k_{1},\ldots k_{r}))=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}f_{1},\ldots f_{r-1}\in\mathbb{F}_{q}[T]^{+}\\
\deg f_{i}\equiv k_{i}\bmod n\end{subarray}}x_{1}^{\deg f_{1}}\cdots x_{r-1}^{\deg f_{r-1}}S^{k_{r},n}\sum_{\ell_{r}=1}^{n_{r}}\left(\frac{qx_{r}}{g}\right)^{K}\Gamma_{k_{r},\ell_{r}}(x_{r},K)D(\frac{g^{2}}{q^{2}x_{r}},(f_{1},\ldots f_{r-1}),\ell_{r})\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}f_{1},\ldots f_{r-1}\in\mathbb{F}_{q}[T]^{+}\\
\deg f_{i}\equiv k_{i}\bmod n\end{subarray}}x_{1}^{\deg f_{1}}\cdots x_{r-1}^{\deg f_{r-1}}\sum_{\ell_{r}=1}^{n_{r}}\sum_{j=0}^{n}\left(S^{k_{r}-j,n}\left(\frac{qx_{r}}{g}\right)^{K}\Gamma_{k_{r},\ell_{r}}(x_{r},K)\right)\left(S^{j,n}D(\frac{g^{2}}{q^{2}x_{r}},(f_{1},\ldots f_{r-1}),\ell_{r})\right).\end{split}

Because
D(\frac{g^{2}}{q^{2}x_{r}},(f_{1},\ldots f_{r-1}),\ell_{r})
is a rational function whose power series expression only contains powers of
x_{r}
congruent to
-\ell_{r}
modulo
n_{r}
, the terms of the
j
sum vanish except when
j\equiv-\ell_{r}\bmod n_{r}
. We can thus reparametrize the double sum over
j
and
\ell_{r}\bmod n_{r}
as a single sum over
\ell_{r}\bmod n
, with
j=-\ell_{r}
. This leads to the formula

\begin{split}Z(\vec{x},(k_{1},\ldots k_{r}))=&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}f_{1},\ldots f_{r-1}\in\mathbb{F}_{q}[T]^{+}\\
\deg f_{i}\equiv k_{i}\bmod n\end{subarray}}\left(\left(\frac{qx_{r}}{g}\right)^{n_{r1}}x_{1}\right)^{\deg f_{1}}\cdots\left(\left(\frac{qx_{r}}{g}\right)^{n_{r\,(r-1)}}x_{r-1}\right)^{\deg f_{r-1}}\\
&\sum_{\ell_{r}=1}^{n}\left(S^{k_{r}+\ell_{r}-K,n}\Gamma_{k_{r},\ell_{r}}(x_{r},K)\right)\left(S^{-\ell_{r},n}D(\frac{g^{2}}{q^{2}x_{r}},(f_{1},\ldots f_{r-1}),\ell_{r})\right)\end{split}

which is equivalent to the desired vector functional equation

(Z(\vec{x},(k_{1},\ldots k_{r})))_{k_{r}}=\left(E_{n_{r}}^{n}\Gamma_{k_{r},\ell_{r}}(x_{r},K)\right)_{k_{r},\ell_{r}}(Z(\sigma_{r}(\vec{x}),(k_{1},\ldots k_{r-1},\ell_{r})))_{\ell_{r}}.

(3.25)

This completes the proof when
i=r
.

For an arbitrary
i
with
\gcd(n,M_{ii}+n/2)|M_{ij}
, for all
j
, let
\rho
be the matrix representing the permutation
(i\,r\,r-1\,\ldots\,i+1)
. If
a(f_{1},\ldots f_{r};M)
are axiomatic coefficients for the matrix
M
, then we have

a(\rho(f_{1},\ldots f_{r});\rho M\rho^{-1})=a(f_{1},\ldots f_{r};M)\chi(-1)^{\deg f_{i}(M_{i\,(i+1)}\deg f_{i+1}+\cdots+M_{ir}\deg f_{r})}.

It is straightforward to check that this expression satisfies the axioms for the conjugate matrix
\rho M\rho^{-1}
. The power of
\chi(-1)
is introduced because of the reciprocity law, since the order of the
f_{j}
terms changes. Thus

(\chi(-1)^{k_{i}v_{i}}Z(\vec{x},(k_{1},\ldots k_{r});M))_{k_{i}}=Z(\rho(\vec{x}),\rho(k_{1},\ldots k_{r});\rho M\rho^{-1})_{k_{i}}.

The
\sigma_{r}
functional equation on the right side corresponds to a
\sigma_{i}
functional equation on the left. To obtain the
\sigma_{i}
functional equation for
Z(\vec{x},(k_{1},\ldots k_{r});M)_{k_{i}}
on its own, conjugate the scattering matrix by a diagonal matrix whose entry at the
(k_{i},k_{i})
position is
\chi(-1)^{k_{i}v_{i}}
. This introduces the powers of
\chi(-1)^{v_{i}}
into the formulas for the scattering matrix entries.
∎

We also have the analogous local functional equation.

Theorem 3.11
.

Assume that
q\equiv 1\bmod 4
,
n
is even, and
M
is such that
\gcd(n,M_{ii}+n/2)|M_{ij}
, for all
j
. The vector
(Z_{\pi}(\vec{x},(k_{1},\ldots k_{r}))_{k_{i}}
satisfies a functional equation

(Z_{\pi}(\vec{x},(k_{1},\ldots k_{r}))_{k_{i}}=(\chi^{v_{i}(k_{i}+\ell_{i})\deg\pi}(-1)E_{n_{i}}^{n}\Gamma_{\pi,k_{i},\ell_{i}}(x_{i},K))_{k_{i},\ell_{i}}(Z_{\pi}(\sigma_{i}(\vec{x}),(k_{1},\ldots k_{i-1},\ell_{i},k_{i+1,\ldots k_{r}}))_{\ell_{i}}

(3.26)

where
\left(E_{n_{i}}^{n}\Gamma_{\pi,k,\ell}(x,K)\right)_{k,\ell}
is the
n\times n
scattering matrix obtained by applying the expansion operation above to the scattering matrix from Proposition
3.2
and Theorem
3.9
, namely

\begin{split}E_{n_{i}}^{n}\Gamma_{k,\ell}(x,K)=&q^{(n-n_{i}-(n-n_{i}+1+K-k-\ell)\%n+(1+K-k-\ell)\%n_{i})\deg\pi/n_{i}}\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{(n-n_{i}+1-(n-n_{i}+1+K-k-\ell)\%n)\deg\pi}\\
&\frac{1-q^{\deg\pi}}{1-q^{n\deg\pi/n_{i}}\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n\deg\pi}}\end{split}

when
k\equiv\ell\bmod n_{i}
,

\begin{split}&E_{n_{i}}^{n}\Gamma_{k,\ell}(x,K)=(\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{\ell(1+K)\deg\pi}(-1)\frac{g_{(\xi\chi^{M_{rr}})^{2k-K-1}}(1,\pi)}{q^{\deg\pi}}\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{\deg\pi}\\
&\frac{q^{((k+\ell-K-1)\%n)\deg\pi/n_{i}}\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{((k+\ell-K-1)\%n)\deg\pi}-q^{((k+\ell-K-1+n_{i})\%n)\deg\pi/n_{i}}\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{(-n_{i}+(k+\ell-K-1+n_{i})\%n)\deg\pi}}{1-q^{n\deg\pi/n_{i}}\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{n\deg\pi}}\end{split}

for
\ell\equiv 1+K-k\bmod n_{i}
, and all other entries equal to
0
. In addition, there are special entries when
k\equiv\ell\equiv 1+K-k-n_{i}\bmod n
. In this case

\Gamma_{k,\ell}(x,K)=\left(\frac{qx}{g}\right)^{(1-n_{i})\deg\pi}.

The proof is the same as that of Theorem
3.10
, using the local functional equation in Theorem
3.9
.

4
Dirichlet Functional Equation

4.1
Single Variable Functional Equation

In this section, we prove a functional equation for axiomatic multiple Dirichlet series based on functional equations of Dirichlet L-functions. Unlike the functional equation constructed in the previous section, this functional equation usually relates multiple Dirichlet series constructed from two different matrices. The one exception is when the relevant Dirichlet characters are quadratic, where this functional equation relates a multiple Dirichlet series to itself, and matches the previously constructed functional equation. This special case will be discussed at the end of this section.

Again, we begin with a single-variable subseries and its functional equation. We still take
q
an odd prime power (with no assumption that
q\equiv 1\bmod 4
in this section),
\chi
a character of even order
n
, and
M=(M_{ij})
an
r\times r
integer matrix
M=(M_{ij})
, whose entries we assume lie in
[0,n)
. For this section, we make the assumption that
M_{rr}=0
. This implies
n_{r}=2
, each
n_{ri}=0
or
1
, and
p_{ri}=(-M_{ri})\%\frac{n}{2}
.

The formula for coefficients
a(f_{1},\ldots f_{r})
with
f_{1}\cdots f_{r-1}
squarefree becomes substantially simpler in this case. It matches formula (
1.6
), which had the stronger hypothesis of
f_{1}\cdots f_{r}
squarefree.

Proposition 4.1
.

Assume
M_{rr}=0
. Fix
f_{1},\ldots,f_{r-1}
with
f_{1}\cdots f_{r-1}
squarefree. Then for all
f_{r}
,

a(f_{1},\ldots f_{r};q,\chi,M)=\prod_{i=1}^{r-1}\left(\frac{f_{i}^{\prime}}{f_{i}}\right)_{\chi}^{M_{ii}}\,\prod_{i=1}^{r-1}\prod_{j=i+1}^{r}\left(\frac{f_{i}}{f_{j}}\right)_{\chi}^{M_{ij}}.

(4.1)

Proof.

We specialize Proposition
2.6
. Recall the notation of that Proposition:
F=f_{1}^{n_{ri}}\cdots f_{r-1}^{n_{r\,(r-1)}}
and

\varepsilon=\prod_{i=1}^{r-1}\left(\frac{f_{i}^{\prime}}{f_{i}}\right)_{\chi}^{M_{ii}}\,\prod_{i=1}^{r-2}\prod_{j=i+1}^{r-1}\left(\frac{f_{i}}{f_{j}}\right)_{\chi}^{M_{ij}}.

If
M_{rr}=0
, then
F
is squarefree and
f_{1}^{p_{r1}}\cdots f_{r-1}^{p_{r\,(r-1)}}
is
n
-th power free. Moreover, if a prime
\pi
divides
F
and
f_{r}
, then by formula (
2.18
),
a(f_{1},\ldots f_{r})
vanishes. Thus, the formula holds for
\gcd(F,f_{r})\neq 1
and we may assume that
\gcd(F,f_{r})=1
.

The Gauss sums in equation (
2.10
) are quadratic in this case. We recall the following evaluations, for
\xi
the unique character of order
2
on
\mathbb{F}_{q}^{*}
. For
\gcd(F,f_{r})=1
, we have
g_{\xi}(F,f_{r})=\left(\frac{F}{f_{r}}\right)_{\xi}^{-1}g_{\xi}(1,f_{r})
. Gauss showed that

g_{\xi}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\sqrt{q}&q\equiv 1\bmod 4\\
i\sqrt{q}&q\equiv-1\bmod 4\end{array}\right..

(4.2)

And finally, for arbitrary
f_{r}
,

g_{\xi}(1,f_{r})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\xi(-1)^{\deg f_{r}(\deg f_{r}-1)/2}g_{\xi}^{\deg f_{r}}&f_{r}\text{ squarefree}\\
0&\text{ otherwise}\end{array}\right..

The first case is by Lemma
2.2
, and the second is an elementary evaluation. Therefore in equation (
2.10
), the only term which contributes to the sum over
u
is where
f_{r}/u^{2}
is squarefree. Fixing this
u
value, we obtain

\begin{split}a(f_{1},\ldots f_{r})&=\varepsilon\left(\frac{f_{1}^{p_{r1}}\cdots f_{r-1}^{p_{r\,(r-1)}}}{f_{r}}\right)^{-1}_{\chi}\left(\frac{F}{f_{r}}\right)_{\xi}^{-1}\frac{\xi(-1)^{\deg f_{r}(\deg f_{r}-1)/2}}{g_{\xi}^{\deg f_{r}}}q^{\deg u}g_{\xi}(1,f_{r}/u^{2})\\
&=\varepsilon\left(\frac{f_{1}^{p_{r1}}\cdots f_{r-1}^{p_{r\,(r-1)}}}{f_{r}}\right)^{-1}_{\chi}\left(\frac{F}{f_{r}}\right)_{\xi}^{-1}\frac{q^{\deg u}\xi(-1)^{\deg u}}{g_{\xi}^{2\deg u}}.\end{split}

The last factor is
1
by (
4.2
). For each
i
, we have
\chi^{-p_{ri}}\xi^{-n_{ri}}=\chi^{M_{ri}}
by equation (
2.12
). This leads to

\left(\frac{f_{1}^{p_{r1}}\cdots f_{r-1}^{p_{r\,(r-1)}}}{f_{r}}\right)^{-1}_{\chi}\left(\frac{F}{f_{r}}\right)_{\xi}^{-1}=\prod_{i=1}^{r-1}\left(\frac{f_{i}}{f_{r}}\right)_{\chi}^{M_{ri}}

which completes the proof.
∎

We will set
e_{ij}=1
if
M_{ij}\neq 0
,
e_{ij}=0
if
M_{ij}=0
. For the rest of this section, we set
\widetilde{F}=f_{1}^{M_{1r}}\cdots f_{r-1}^{M_{(r-1)\,r}}
, and
F=f_{1}^{e_{1r}}\cdots f_{r-1}^{e_{(r-1)\,r}}
. Note that this definition of
F
does not match the definition used in Sections
2
,
3
, or in the proof of Proposition
4.1
. The reason for the redefinition is that this
F
plays the same role in the Dirichlet functional equation that the previous
F
played in the Kubota functional equation.

For fixed
\vec{f}=(f_{1},\ldots f_{r-1})
and prime
\pi
, we define

\displaystyle D(x,\vec{f})=\sum_{f_{r}\in\mathbb{F}_{q}[T]^{+}}a(\vec{f},f_{r})x^{\deg f_{r}}

(4.3)

\displaystyle D_{\pi}(x,\vec{f})=\sum_{d\geq 0}a(\vec{f},\pi^{d})x^{d\deg\pi}.

(4.4)

Then for
f_{1}\cdots f_{r-1}
squarefree, Proposition
4.1
implies that

D(x,\vec{f})=\varepsilon L\left(x,\left(\frac{\widetilde{F}}{*}\right)_{\chi}\right).

(4.5)

Where
L\left(x,\left(\frac{\widetilde{F}}{*}\right)_{\chi}\right)
is the Dirichlet
L
-function

L\left(x,\left(\frac{\widetilde{F}}{*}\right)_{\chi}\right)=\sum_{f_{r}\in\mathbb{F}_{q}[T]^{+}}\left(\frac{\widetilde{F}}{f_{r}}\right)_{\chi}x^{\deg f_{r}}.

By quadratic reciprocity,
L\left(x,\left(\frac{\widetilde{F}}{*}\right)_{\chi}\right)=L\left(\chi(-1)^{\deg\widetilde{F}}x,\left(\frac{*}{\widetilde{F}}\right)_{\chi}\right)
.
We are assuming that all
M_{ij}
are between
0
and
n-1
, so
\widetilde{F}
is
n
-th power free.
F
, which is the conductor of the
L
-function, is squarefree. Unless
\widetilde{F}=1
,
L\left(x,\left(\frac{*}{\widetilde{F}}\right)_{\chi}\right)
is a polynomial of degree
\deg F-1
. In the special case
\widetilde{F}=1
,
L\left(x,\left(\frac{*}{\widetilde{F}}\right)_{\chi}\right)=(1-qx)^{-1}
is the function field zeta function.

Locally, Proposition
4.1
implies that

D_{\pi}(x,\vec{f})=\varepsilon\sum_{d\geq 0}\left(\frac{\widetilde{F}}{\pi}\right)_{\chi}^{d}x^{d\deg\pi}=\frac{\varepsilon}{1-\left(\frac{\widetilde{F}}{\pi}\right)_{\chi}x^{\deg\pi}}=\frac{\varepsilon}{1-\left(\frac{\pi}{\widetilde{F}}\right)_{\chi}(\chi(-1)^{\deg\widetilde{F}}x)^{\deg\pi}}.

(4.6)

This becomes just
\varepsilon
if
\pi|\widetilde{F}
.

The Dirichlet
L
-functions have a symmetry in
x\mapsto\frac{1}{qx}
. Let
b=1
if
n|\deg\widetilde{F}
and
b=0
otherwise, so that the Euler factor at infinity is
(1-x)^{-b}
. Then

(1-x)^{-b}L\left(x,\left(\frac{*}{\widetilde{F}}\right)_{\chi}\right)=\omega_{1}(\sqrt{q}x)^{\deg F-1-b}\left(1-\frac{1}{qx}\right)^{-b}L\left(\frac{1}{qx},\left(\frac{*}{\widetilde{F}}\right)_{\bar{\chi}}\right)

(4.7)

where the root number
\omega_{1}
is

\omega_{1}=\left(\sum_{\nu\bmod F}\left(\frac{\nu}{\widetilde{F}}\right)_{\chi}\exp\left(\nu/F\right)\right)\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}q^{(1-\deg F)/2}g_{\chi^{\deg\widetilde{F}}}^{-1}&n\nmid\deg\widetilde{F}\\
q^{-\deg F/2}&n|\deg\widetilde{F}\end{array}\right..

We can expand the root number further using the twisted multiplicativity of of the Gauss sum:

\begin{split}\sum_{\nu\bmod F}\left(\frac{\nu}{\widetilde{F}}\right)_{\chi}\exp\left(\nu/F\right)&=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq r-1\\
M_{ir}\neq 0\end{subarray}}g_{\chi^{M_{ir}}}(1,f_{i})\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i<j\leq r-1\\
M_{ir},M_{jr}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{f_{i}}{f_{j}^{M_{jr}}}\right)_{\chi}\left(\frac{f_{j}}{f_{i}^{M_{ir}}}\right)_{\chi}\\
&=\omega\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq r-1\\
M_{ir}\neq 0\end{subarray}}g_{\chi^{M_{ir}}}^{\deg f_{i}}\left(\frac{f_{i}^{\prime}}{f_{i}}\right)_{\chi}^{M_{ir}+n/2}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i<j\leq r-1\\
M_{ir},M_{jr}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{f_{i}}{f_{j}}\right)_{\chi}^{M_{ir}+M_{jr}}\end{split}

where

\omega=\omega(\vec{f};M)=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq r-1\\
M_{ir}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\xi(-1)^{\deg f_{i}(\deg f_{i}-1)/2}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i<j\leq r-1\\
M_{ir},M_{jr}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\chi(-1)^{(\deg f_{i})(\deg f_{j})M_{ir}}=\pm 1.

(4.8)

Introduce a new
r\times r
integer matrix
M^{\prime}
with entries as follows:
M^{\prime}_{rr}=M_{rr}=0
,
M^{\prime}_{ir}=-M_{ir}
for
i\neq r
,
M^{\prime}_{ii}=M_{ii}+M_{ir}+\frac{n}{2}
for
i\neq r
and
M_{ir}\neq 0
,
M^{\prime}_{ii}=M_{ii}
for
M_{ir}=0
,
M^{\prime}_{ij}=M_{ij}
for
i\neq j\neq r
if
M_{ir}=0
or
M_{jr}=0
,
M^{\prime}_{ij}=M_{ij}+M_{ir}+M_{jr}
for
i\neq j\neq r
if
M_{ir}\neq 0
and
M_{jr}\neq 0
. By Proposition
4.1
the axiomatic coefficients
a(f_{1},\ldots f_{r-1},f_{r};q,\chi,M^{\prime})
constructed from the matrix
M^{\prime}
are as follows

a(f_{1},\ldots f_{r-1},f_{r};M^{\prime})=\varepsilon\prod_{i=1}^{r-1}\left(\frac{f_{i}}{f_{r}}\right)_{\chi}^{-M_{ir}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq r-1\\
M_{ir}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{f_{i}^{\prime}}{f_{i}}\right)_{\chi}^{M_{ir}+n/2}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i<j\leq r-1\\
M_{ir},M_{jr}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{f_{i}}{f_{j}}\right)_{\chi}^{M_{ir}+M_{jr}}.

And the single-variable series for
M^{\prime}
is

D(x,\vec{f};M^{\prime})=\epsilon\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq r-1\\
M_{ir}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{f_{i}^{\prime}}{f_{i}}\right)_{\chi}^{M_{ir}+n/2}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i<j\leq r-1\\
M_{ir},M_{jr}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{f_{i}}{f_{j}}\right)_{\chi}^{M_{ir}+M_{jr}}L\left(\chi(-1)^{\deg\widetilde{F}}x,\left(\frac{*}{\widetilde{F}}\right)_{\bar{\chi}}\right).

Then the above calculations lead to the following functional equation, which relates
D(x,\vec{f};M)
to
D(x,\vec{f};M^{\prime})

Proposition 4.2
.

Suppose that
M_{rr}=0
and
f_{1}\cdots f_{r-1}
is squarefree. Then
D(x,\vec{f};M)
is a rational function of
x
with denominator dividing
1-qx
if
\widetilde{F}
is an
n
th power in
\mathbb{F}_{q}[T]
, and a polynomial otherwise. It satisfies the functional equation

\begin{split}D(x,\vec{f};M)=&\omega\,g_{\chi^{\deg\widetilde{F}}}^{b-1}\,(\chi(-1)^{\deg\widetilde{F}}x)^{\deg F-1}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq r-1\\
M_{ir}\neq 0\end{subarray}}g_{\chi^{M_{ir}}}^{\deg f_{i}}\,\left(\frac{x-1}{1-qx}\right)^{b}D\left(\frac{1}{qx},\vec{f};M^{\prime}\right)\end{split}

(4.9)

with
\omega
defined in equation (
4.8
).

Since we are assuming
f_{1}\cdots f_{r-1}
is squarefree, the only way
\widetilde{F}
can be an
n
th power is if it is
1
. But we will show in Theorem
3.9
that the statement of the proposition actually holds for all
f
, without the squarefree hypothesis.
F
will still be the conductor, determining the degree of the polynomial, even when it is not squarefree.

Next we give the corresponding local functional equation. The local series
D_{\pi}(x,\vec{f};M^{\prime})
, for
f_{1}\cdots f_{r-1}
squarefree, is as follows:

D_{\pi}(x,\vec{f},M^{\prime})=\frac{\varepsilon\prod\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq r-1\\
M_{ir}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{f_{i}^{\prime}}{f_{i}}\right)_{\chi}^{M_{ir}+n/2}\prod\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i<j\leq r-1\\
M_{ir},M_{jr}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{f_{i}}{f_{j}}\right)_{\chi}^{M_{ir}+M_{jr}}}{1-\left(\frac{\pi}{\widetilde{F}}\right)^{-1}_{\chi}(\chi(-1)^{\deg\widetilde{F}}x)^{\deg\pi}}.

The denominator becomes
1
if
\pi|\widetilde{F}
.

We will state the local functional equation only when
\vec{f}
consists of powers of
\pi
.

Proposition 4.3
.

Suppose that
M_{rr}=0
and
f_{1}\cdots f_{r-1}=1
or
\pi
. Then
D_{\pi}(x,\vec{f};M)
is a rational function of
x
with denominator dividing
1-x^{\deg\pi}
if
n|v_{\pi}\widetilde{F}
, and a polynomial otherwise. It satisfies the functional equation

\begin{split}D_{\pi}(x,\vec{f};M)&=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq r-1\\
M_{ir}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{\pi^{\prime}}{\pi}\right)_{\chi}^{-v_{\pi}f_{i}(M_{ir}+n/2)}\omega_{\pi}\left(\frac{-(-g_{\chi^{v_{\pi}\widetilde{F}}})^{\deg\pi}}{q^{\deg\pi}}\right)^{b_{\pi}-1}(\chi(-1)^{v_{\pi}\widetilde{F}}qx)^{\deg F-\deg\pi}\\
&\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq r-1\\
M_{ir}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{-(-g_{\chi^{M_{ir}}})^{\deg\pi}}{q^{\deg\pi}}\right)^{v_{\pi}f_{i}}\left(\frac{(qx)^{\deg\pi}-1}{1-x^{\deg\pi}}\right)^{b_{\pi}}D_{\pi}\left(\frac{1}{qx},\vec{f};M^{\prime}\right)\end{split}

(4.10)

where
b_{\pi}=1
if
n|v_{\pi}\widetilde{F}
and
0
otherwise, and

\omega_{\pi}=\omega_{\pi}(\vec{f};M)=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq r-1\\
M_{ir}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\xi(-1)^{\deg\pi(v_{\pi}f_{i})(v_{\pi}f_{i}-1)/2}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i<j\leq r-1\\
M_{ir},M_{jr}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\chi(-1)^{\deg\pi(v_{\pi}f_{i})(v_{\pi}f_{j})M_{ir}}=\pm 1.

(4.11)

Again, Theorem
4.6
will establish that this statement holds for all
\vec{f}=(\pi^{d_{1}},\ldots\pi^{d_{r-1}})
. Proposition
4.3
is checked by direct computation when
\vec{f}=(1,\ldots 1)
or
\vec{f}=(1,\ldots\pi,\ldots 1)
. Some terms in Equation (
4.10
) trivialize in these cases, but this is the correct form of the functional equation for general
\vec{f}
.

Note that the form of the functional equation (
4.9
) depends on
\deg f_{1},\ldots\deg f_{r-1}
but not on
f_{1},\ldots f_{r-1}
themselves. Furthermore, the local and global functional equations (
4.9
) and (
4.10
) are compatible under the axioms. Axiom
5
gives the change of variables between these two functional equations. Axiom
3
explains the extra term involving
\left(\frac{\pi^{\prime}}{\pi}\right)_{\chi}
in (
4.10
), which arises from the discrepancy between
M^{\prime}
and
M
.

The series
D(x,\vec{f})
has an Euler product expression. We can see this from the explicit formulas above when
f_{1}\cdots f_{r-1}
is squarefree, but it holds for arbitrary
\vec{f}
because of the twisted multiplicativity axiom with
M_{rr}=0
. We will need the analogue of Lemma
3.4
to separate out the contribution of one prime. Let

D^{(\pi)}(x,\vec{f},\pi^{\ell};q,\chi M)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}f_{r}\in\mathbb{F}_{q}[T]^{+}\\
\pi\nmid f_{r}\end{subarray}}a(\vec{f},f_{r};q,\chi,M)\left(\frac{\pi^{\ell}}{f_{r}}\right)_{\chi}x^{\deg f_{r}}

(4.12)

and for
\pi\nmid h
, let

D_{\pi}(x,\vec{f},h;q,\chi,M)=\sum_{d\geq 0}a(\vec{f},\pi^{d};q,\chi,M)\left(\frac{h}{\pi^{d}}\right)_{\chi}x^{d\deg\pi}.

(4.13)

Note that
D_{\pi}(x,\vec{f},h)=D_{\pi}\left(\left(\frac{h}{\pi}\right)_{\chi}^{1/\deg\pi}x,\vec{f}\right)
, so this latter expression is just a change of variables of the
D_{\pi}(x,\vec{f})
defined earlier.

Lemma 4.4
.

For any prime
\pi
, we have the following equality

D(x,\vec{f})=\varepsilon_{\pi}D_{\pi}(x,\vec{f}_{\pi},\widetilde{F}^{(\pi)})D^{(\pi)}(x,\vec{f}^{(\pi)},\widetilde{F}_{\pi})

(4.14)

where
\varepsilon_{\pi}=\varepsilon_{\pi}(\vec{f};M)
is defined in equation (
3.4
).

Proof.

By the twisted multiplicativity axiom, we have

a(f_{1},\ldots f_{r-1},f_{r})=\varepsilon_{\pi}a((f_{1})_{\pi},\ldots(f_{r-1})_{\pi},(f_{r})_{\pi})\left(\frac{\widetilde{F}^{(\pi)}}{(f_{r})_{\pi}}\right)_{\chi}a(f_{1}^{(\pi)},\ldots f_{r-1}^{(\pi)},f_{r}^{(\pi)})\left(\frac{\widetilde{F}_{\pi}}{f_{r}^{(\pi)}}\right)_{\chi}.

Then the lemma follows by splitting the sum over
f_{r}
in
D(x,\vec{f})
into a sum over
(f_{r})_{\pi}
and a sum over
f_{r}^{(\pi)}
.
∎

For a given
r\times r
matrix
M
, we will employ an augmented matrix
M_{v}
with an additional zeroth row and column. The extra entries are given by
M_{00}=\cdots=M_{0\,(r-1)}=0
and
M_{0r}=v\%n
.

Proposition 4.5
.

Suppose
D(x,(\pi,\vec{f}^{(\pi)});M_{v_{\pi}\widetilde{F}})
is a polynomial, or a rational function with denominator dividing
1-qx
if
\widetilde{F}
is an
n
th power, and the functional equation (
4.9
) holds for this series. Further, suppose
D_{\pi}(x,\vec{f}_{\pi};M)
is a polynomial, or a rational function with denominator dividing
1-x^{\deg\pi}
if
\widetilde{F}_{\pi}
is an
n
th power, and the local functional equation (
4.10
) holds for this series. Then
D(x,\vec{f};M)
is a polynomial, or a rational function with denominator dividing
1-qx
if
\widetilde{F}
is an
n
th power, and the functional equation (
4.9
) holds for this series.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.4
we have

D(x,\vec{f};M)=\varepsilon_{\pi}(\vec{f};M)D_{\pi}(x,\vec{f}_{\pi},\widetilde{F}^{(\pi)};M)D^{(\pi)}(x,\vec{f}^{(\pi)},\widetilde{F}_{\pi};M)

and also

\begin{split}D(x,(\pi,\vec{f}^{(\pi)});M_{v_{\pi}\widetilde{F}})&=D_{\pi}(x,(\pi,\vec{1}),\widetilde{F}^{(\pi)};M_{v_{\pi}\widetilde{F}})D^{(\pi)}(x,(1,\vec{f}^{(\pi)}),\widetilde{F}_{\pi};M_{v_{\pi}\widetilde{F}})\\
&=\left(1-\left(\frac{\widetilde{F}^{(\pi)}}{\pi}\right)_{\chi}x^{\deg\pi}\right)^{-b_{\pi}}D^{(\pi)}(x,\vec{f}^{(\pi)},\widetilde{F}_{\pi};M).\end{split}

In the second line,
b_{\pi}
is
1
if
n
divides
v_{\pi}\widetilde{F}
and zero otherwise. The local series at
\pi
is evaluated as in equation (
4.6
), while the series away from
\pi
simplifies because the first parameter is
1
.

Therefore,

D(x,\vec{f};M)=\varepsilon_{\pi}(\vec{f};M)\left(1-\left(\frac{\widetilde{F}^{(\pi)}}{\pi}\right)_{\chi}x^{\deg\pi}\right)^{b_{\pi}}D_{\pi}(x,\vec{f}_{\pi},\widetilde{F}^{(\pi)};M)D(x,(\pi,\vec{f}^{(\pi)});M_{v_{\pi}\widetilde{F}}).

(4.15)

Since
D_{\pi}(x,\vec{f}_{\pi},\widetilde{F}^{(\pi)};M)=D_{\pi}\left(\left(\frac{\widetilde{F}^{(\pi)}}{\pi}\right)_{\chi}^{1/\deg\pi}x,\vec{f}_{\pi};M\right)
, our hypothesis on the rationality of
D_{\pi}(x,\vec{f}_{\pi};M)
implies that
\left(1-\left(\frac{\widetilde{F}^{(\pi)}}{\pi}\right)_{\chi}x^{\deg\pi}\right)^{b_{\pi}}D_{\pi}(x,\vec{f}_{\pi},\widetilde{F}^{(\pi)};M)
is a polynomial. Thus our hypothesis on the rationality of
D(x,(\pi,\vec{f}^{(\pi)});M_{v_{\pi}\widetilde{F}})
implies that
D(x,\vec{f};M)
is a polynomial, or a rational function with denominator dividing
1-qx
if
\widetilde{F}
is an
n
th power.

To prove the functional equation, we would like to compare equation (
4.15
) to a similar expression for
D\left(\frac{1}{qx},\vec{f};M^{\prime}\right)
. By Lemma
4.4
, we have

D\left(\frac{1}{qx},\vec{f};M^{\prime}\right)=\varepsilon_{\pi}(\vec{f};M^{\prime})D_{\pi}\left(\frac{1}{qx},\vec{f}_{\pi},(\widetilde{F}^{(\pi)})^{-1};M^{\prime}\right)D^{(\pi)}\left(\frac{1}{qx},\vec{f}^{(\pi)},\widetilde{F}_{\pi}^{-1};M^{\prime}\right).

The matrix
(M_{v_{\pi}\widetilde{F}})^{\prime}
is
M^{\prime}
augmented with a zeroth row and column. If
v_{\pi}\widetilde{F}\equiv 0\bmod n
, the extra entries are all
0
. If
v_{\pi}\widetilde{F}\not\equiv 0\bmod n
, then the extra entries are
((M_{v_{\pi}\widetilde{F}})^{\prime})_{00}=v_{\pi}\widetilde{F}+n/2
,
((M_{v_{\pi}\widetilde{F}})^{\prime})_{0i}=0
if
M_{ir}=0
and
((M_{v_{\pi}\widetilde{F}})^{\prime})_{0i}=v_{\pi}\widetilde{F}+M_{ir}
otherwise, and
((M_{v_{\pi}(\widetilde{F}})^{\prime})_{0r}=-v_{\pi}\widetilde{F}
. By another application of Lemma
4.4
, we have

\begin{split}&D\left(\frac{1}{qx},(\pi,\vec{f}^{(\pi)});(M_{v_{\pi}\widetilde{F}})^{\prime}\right)\\
&=\varepsilon_{\pi}((\pi,\vec{f}^{(\pi)});(M_{v_{\pi}\widetilde{F}})^{\prime})D_{\pi}\left(\frac{1}{qx},(\pi,\vec{1}),(\widetilde{F}^{(\pi)})^{-1};(M_{-v_{\pi}\widetilde{F}})^{\prime}\right)D^{(\pi)}\left(\frac{1}{qx},(1,\vec{f}^{(\pi)}),\widetilde{F}_{\pi}^{-1};(M_{-v_{\pi}\widetilde{F}})^{\prime}\right)\\
&=\varepsilon_{\pi}((\pi,\vec{f}^{(\pi)});(M_{v_{\pi}\widetilde{F}})^{\prime})\left(\frac{\pi^{\prime}}{\pi}\right)_{\chi}^{(v_{\pi}\widetilde{F}+n/2)(1-b_{\pi})}\left(1-\left(\frac{\widetilde{F}^{(\pi)}}{\pi}\right)_{\chi}^{-1}(qx)^{-\deg\pi}\right)^{-b_{\pi}}D^{(\pi)}\left(\frac{1}{qx},\vec{f}^{(\pi)},\widetilde{F}_{\pi}^{-1};M^{\prime}\right).\end{split}

Again, the local series at
\pi
is evaluated as in equation (
4.6
), while the series away from
\pi
simplifies because the first parameter is
1
.

Therefore,

\begin{split}D\left(\frac{1}{qx},\vec{f};M^{\prime}\right)=&\frac{\varepsilon_{\pi}(\vec{f};M^{\prime})}{\varepsilon_{\pi}((\pi,\vec{f}^{(\pi)});(M_{v_{\pi}\widetilde{F}})^{\prime})}\left(\frac{\pi^{\prime}}{\pi}\right)_{\chi}^{(v_{\pi}\widetilde{F}+n/2)(b_{\pi}-1)}\left(1-\left(\frac{\widetilde{F}^{(\pi)}}{\pi}\right)_{\chi}^{-1}(qx)^{-\deg\pi}\right)^{b_{\pi}}\\
&D_{\pi}\left(\frac{1}{qx},\vec{f}_{\pi},(\widetilde{F}^{(\pi)})^{-1};M^{\prime}\right)D\left(\frac{1}{qx},\left(\pi,\vec{f}^{(\pi)}\right);(M_{v_{\pi}\widetilde{F}})^{\prime}\right).\end{split}

(4.16)

The known functional equation of
D_{\pi}(x,\vec{f}_{\pi},\widetilde{F}^{(\pi)};M)
is as follows:

\begin{split}&\frac{D_{\pi}(x,\vec{f}_{\pi},\widetilde{F}^{(\pi)};M)}{D_{\pi}\left(\frac{1}{qx},\vec{f}_{\pi},(\widetilde{F}^{(\pi)})^{-1};M^{\prime}\right)}=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq r-1\\
M_{ir}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{\pi^{\prime}}{\pi}\right)_{\chi}^{-v_{\pi}f_{i}(M_{ir}+n/2)}\omega_{\pi}(\vec{f}_{\pi};M)\left(\frac{-(-g_{\chi^{v_{\pi}\widetilde{F}}})^{\deg\pi}}{q^{\deg\pi}}\right)^{b_{\pi}-1}\\
&\left(\frac{\widetilde{F}^{(\pi)}}{\pi}\right)_{\chi}^{v_{\pi}F-1}(\chi(-1)^{v_{\pi}\widetilde{F}}qx)^{\deg F_{\pi}-\deg\pi}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq r-1\\
M_{ir}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{-(-g_{\chi^{M_{ir}}})^{\deg\pi}}{q^{\deg\pi}}\right)^{v_{\pi}f_{i}}\left(\frac{\left(\frac{\widetilde{F}^{(\pi)}}{\pi}\right)_{\chi}(qx)^{\deg\pi}-1}{1-\left(\frac{\widetilde{F}^{(\pi)}}{\pi}\right)_{\chi}x^{\deg\pi}}\right)^{b_{\pi}}.\end{split}

The known functional equation of
D(x,(\pi,\vec{f}^{(\pi)});M_{v_{\pi}\widetilde{F}})
is as follows:

\begin{split}\frac{D(x,(\pi,\vec{f}^{(\pi)});M_{v_{\pi}\widetilde{F}})}{D\left(\frac{1}{qx},\left(\pi,\vec{f}^{(\pi)}\right);(M_{v_{\pi}\widetilde{F}})^{\prime}\right)}=&\omega((\pi,\vec{f}^{(\pi)});M_{v_{\pi}\widetilde{F}})\,g_{\chi^{\deg\widetilde{F}}}^{b-1}(\chi(-1)^{\deg\widetilde{F}}x)^{\deg F^{(\pi)}+(1-b_{\pi})\deg\pi-1}\\
&g_{\chi^{v_{\pi}\widetilde{F}}}^{\deg\pi(1-b_{\pi})}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq r-1\\
M_{ir}\neq 0\end{subarray}}g_{\chi^{M_{ir}}}^{\deg f_{i}^{(\pi)}}\,\left(\frac{x-1}{1-qx}\right)^{b}\end{split}

where the values of
\deg\widetilde{F}
mod
n
,
b
and
b_{\pi}
are the same for the pair
(\pi,\vec{f}^{(\pi)})
,
M_{v_{\pi}\widetilde{F}}
as they are for
\vec{f}
,
M
.

Dividing Equation (
4.15
) by (
4.16
), applying the local and global functional equations, and algebraically simplifying, we obtain:

\begin{split}\frac{D(x,\vec{f};M)}{D\left(\frac{1}{qx},\vec{f};M^{\prime}\right)}&=\frac{\varepsilon_{\pi}(\vec{f};M)\varepsilon_{\pi}((\pi,\vec{f}^{(\pi)});(M_{v_{\pi}\widetilde{F}})^{\prime})}{\varepsilon_{\pi}(\vec{f};M^{\prime})}\left(\frac{\widetilde{F}^{(\pi)}}{F_{\pi}}\right)_{\chi}\left(\frac{\widetilde{F}^{(\pi)}}{\pi}\right)_{\chi}^{b_{\pi}-1}\\
&\left(\frac{\pi^{\prime}}{\pi}\right)_{\chi}^{(n/2)(1-b_{\pi}-v_{\pi}F)}(-1)^{(\deg\pi+1)(v_{\pi}F+b_{\pi}-1)}\omega_{\pi}(\vec{f}_{\pi};M)\omega((\pi,\vec{f}^{(\pi)});M_{v_{\pi}\widetilde{F}})\\
&\chi(-1)^{(\deg\widetilde{F})(\deg F^{(\pi)}+(1-b_{\pi})\deg\pi-1)+(\deg\widetilde{F}_{\pi})(v_{\pi}F-1)}\\
&x^{\deg F-1}g_{\chi^{\deg\widetilde{F}}}^{b-1}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq r-1\\
M_{ir}\neq 0\end{subarray}}g_{\chi^{M_{ir}}}^{\deg f_{i}}\,\left(\frac{x-1}{1-qx}\right)^{b}.\end{split}

Using
\deg\pi\equiv(\deg\pi)^{2}\bmod 2
, the total power of
\chi(-1)
in the third line is equivalent to

(\deg\widetilde{F})(\deg F-1)+(\deg\widetilde{F}^{(\pi)})((1-b_{\pi})\deg\pi-\deg F_{\pi}).

Using Lemma
2.3
to evaluate
\left(\frac{\pi^{\prime}}{\pi}\right)_{\chi}^{n/2}
, we have

\left(\frac{\pi^{\prime}}{\pi}\right)_{\chi}^{(n/2)(1-b_{\pi}-v_{\pi}F)}(-1)^{(\deg\pi+1)(b_{\pi}-1+v_{\pi}F)}=\xi(-1)^{(b_{\pi}-1+v_{\pi}F)\deg\pi(\deg\pi-1)/2}.

By comparing the entries of
M^{\prime}
and
M
, we find that

\begin{split}\frac{\varepsilon_{\pi}(\vec{f};M)}{\varepsilon_{\pi}(\vec{f};M^{\prime})}&=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq r-1\\
M_{ir}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{f_{i}^{(\pi)}}{(f_{i})_{\pi}}\right)_{\chi}^{-M_{ir}-n/2}\left(\frac{(f_{i})_{\pi}}{f_{i}^{(\pi)}}\right)_{\chi}^{-M_{ir}-n/2}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i<j\leq r-1\\
M_{ir},M_{jr}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{f_{i}^{(\pi)}}{(f_{j})_{\pi}}\right)_{\chi}^{-M_{ir}-M_{jr}}\left(\frac{(f_{i})_{\pi}}{f_{j}^{(\pi)}}\right)_{\chi}^{-M_{ir}-M_{jr}}\\
&=\left(\frac{F^{(\pi)}}{\widetilde{F}_{\pi}}\right)_{\chi}^{-1}\left(\frac{F_{\pi}}{\widetilde{F}^{(\pi)}}\right)_{\chi}^{-1}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq r-1\\
M_{ir}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\xi(-1)^{\deg(f_{i})_{\pi}\,\deg f_{i}^{(\pi)}}\\
&\phantom{=}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i<j\leq r-1\\
M_{ir},M_{jr}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\chi(-1)^{M_{ir}(\deg f_{i}^{(\pi)}\,\deg(f_{j})_{\pi}+\deg(f_{i})_{\pi}\,\deg f_{j}^{(\pi)})}.\end{split}

The last equality is obtained using the reciprocity law and combining the product of residue symbols. Also, from the entries of
(M_{v_{\pi}\widetilde{F}})^{\prime}
, we find that

\varepsilon_{\pi}((\pi,\vec{f}^{(\pi)});(M_{v_{\pi}\widetilde{F}})^{\prime})=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq r-1\\
M_{ir}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{\pi}{f_{i}^{(\pi)}}\right)_{\chi}^{(v_{\pi}\widetilde{F}+M_{ir})(1-b_{\pi})}=\left(\frac{\widetilde{F}_{\pi}}{F^{(\pi)}}\right)_{\chi}\left(\frac{\pi}{\widetilde{F}^{(\pi)}}\right)_{\chi}^{1-b_{\pi}}.

We conclude that

\begin{split}\frac{D(x,\vec{f};M)}{D\left(\frac{1}{qx},\vec{f};M^{\prime}\right)}&=\pm g_{\chi^{\deg\widetilde{F}}}^{b-1}\,(\chi(-1)^{\deg\widetilde{F}}x)^{\deg F-1}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq r-1\\
M_{ir}\neq 0\end{subarray}}g_{\chi^{M_{ir}}}^{\deg f_{i}}\,\left(\frac{x-1}{1-qx}\right)^{b}\end{split}

where the
\pm
symbol is given explicitly by

\begin{split}&\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq r-1\\
M_{ir}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\xi(-1)^{\deg(f_{i})_{\pi}\,\deg f_{i}^{(\pi)}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i<j\leq r-1\\
M_{ir},M_{jr}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\chi(-1)^{M_{ir}(\deg f_{i}^{(\pi)}\,\deg(f_{j})_{\pi}+\deg(f_{i})_{\pi}\,\deg f_{j}^{(\pi)})}\\
&\xi(-1)^{(b_{\pi}-1+v_{\pi}F)\deg\pi(\deg\pi-1)/2}\chi(-1)^{(\deg F^{(\pi)})(\deg\widetilde{F}_{\pi})}\\
&\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq r-1\\
M_{ir}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\xi(-1)^{\deg\pi(v_{\pi}f_{i})(v_{\pi}f_{i}-1)/2}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i<j\leq r-1\\
M_{ir},M_{jr}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\chi(-1)^{\deg\pi(v_{\pi}f_{i})(v_{\pi}f_{j})M_{ir}}\\
&\xi(-1)^{(\deg\pi)(\deg\pi-1)(1-b_{\pi})/2}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq r-1\\
M_{ir}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\chi(-1)^{\deg\pi(\deg f_{i}^{(\pi)})v_{\pi}\widetilde{F}}\\
&\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq r-1\\
M_{ir}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\xi(-1)^{\deg f_{i}^{(\pi)}(\deg f_{i}^{(\pi)}-1)/2}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i<j\leq r-1\\
M_{ir},M_{jr}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\chi(-1)^{(\deg f_{i}^{(\pi)})(\deg f_{j}^{(\pi)})M_{ir}}.\end{split}

(4.17)

The powers of
\chi(-1)
in the second and fourth lines cancel out, and again using
\deg\pi\equiv(\deg\pi)^{2}\bmod 2
, the total power of
\chi(-1)
in (
4.17
) is

\begin{split}&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i<j\leq r-1\\
M_{ir},M_{jr}\neq 0\end{subarray}}M_{ir}(\deg(f_{i})_{\pi}\,\deg(f_{j})_{\pi}+\deg(f_{i})_{\pi}\,\deg f_{j}^{(\pi)}+\deg f_{i}^{(\pi)}\,\deg(f_{j})_{\pi}+\deg f_{i}^{(\pi)})\,\deg f_{j}^{(\pi)}\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i<j\leq r-1\\
M_{ir},M_{jr}\neq 0\end{subarray}}M_{ir}\deg f_{i}\,\deg f_{j}.\end{split}

The power of
\xi(-1)
in the fourth line cancels part of the power in the second line, and again using
\deg\pi\equiv(\deg\pi)^{2}\bmod 2
, the total power of
\xi(-1)
in (
4.17
) is

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq r-1\\
M_{ir}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\deg(f_{i})_{\pi}(\deg(f_{i})_{\pi}-\deg\pi)+\deg(f_{i})_{\pi}(\deg\pi-1)+2\deg(f_{i})_{\pi}\deg f_{i}^{(\pi)}+\deg f_{i}^{(\pi)}(\deg f_{i}^{(\pi)}-1)\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq r-1\\
M_{ir}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\deg f_{i}(\deg f_{i}-1)/2.\end{split}

Thus we find that the expression (
4.17
) matches
\omega(\vec{f};M)
, completing the proof.
∎

We can now prove the functional equations of
D(x,\vec{f};M)
and
D_{\pi}(x,\vec{f};M)
for arbitrary
\vec{f}
.

Theorem 4.6
.

Suppose
M_{rr}=0
. For any
\vec{f}=(f_{1},\ldots f_{r-1})
the series
D(x,\vec{f};M)
is a rational function of
x
with denominator dividing
1-qx
if
\widetilde{F}
is an
n
th power in
F_{q}[T]
and a polynomial otherwise, and satisfies the global functional equation (
4.9
). For any
\pi
prime and
\vec{f}=(\pi^{d_{1}},\ldots\pi^{d_{r-1}})
, the series
D_{\pi}(x,\vec{f};M)
is a rational function of
x
with denominator dividing
1-x^{\deg\pi}
if
\widetilde{F}
is an
n
th power in
F_{q}[T]
and a polynomial otherwise, and satisfies the local functional equation (
4.10
).

Proof.

The proof is by induction on
\deg f_{1}+\cdots+\deg f_{r-1}
and
v_{\pi}f_{1}+\cdots+v_{\pi}f_{r-1}
. Note that the matrix
M
is not fixed in the argument, though we always assume
M_{rr}=0
. At each step in the induction, the global functional equation and rationality statement are proven for all matrices
M
and all
\vec{f}
with
\deg f_{1}+\cdots+\deg f_{r}
fixed, and the local functional equation and rationality statement are proven for all matrices
M
, all
\pi
, and all
\vec{f}
supported on
\pi
with
v_{\pi}f_{1}+\cdots+v_{\pi}f_{r-1}
fixed.

If
\deg f_{1}+\cdots+\deg f_{r-1}=0
or
1
then the global functional equation and rationality statement hold by Proposition
4.2
. If
v_{\pi}f_{1}+\cdots+v_{\pi}f_{r-1}=0
or
1
then the local functional equation and rationality statement hold by Proposition
4.3
.

For the inductive step: suppose that the global functional equation and rationality statement are known for all
M
and all
f
with
\deg f_{1}+\cdots+\deg f_{r-1}<A
, and the local functional equation and rationality statement are known for all
\vec{f}
supported on
\pi
with
v_{\pi}f_{1}+\cdots+v_{\pi}f_{r-1}<A
. If
\vec{f}=(f_{1},\ldots f_{r-1})
has
\deg f_{1}+\cdots+\deg f_{r-1}=A
and
f_{1}\cdots f_{r-1}
is squarefree, then the global functional equation and rationality statement are known for
\vec{f}
by Proposition
4.2
. If
\pi^{2}|f_{1}\cdots f_{r-1}
for some prime
\pi
, we apply Lemma
4.5
. The vector
(\pi,\vec{f}^{(\pi)})
has
\deg\pi+\deg f_{1}^{(\pi)}+\cdots+f_{r-1}^{(\pi)}<A
, so by induction, the global functional equation and rationality statement hold for the series
D(x,(\pi,\vec{f}^{(\pi)});M_{v_{\pi}{\widetilde{F}}})
with extended matrix
M_{v_{\pi}{\widetilde{F}}}
. Moreover, unless
\pi
is linear and each
f_{i}
is a power of
\pi
, we have
v_{\pi}f_{1}+\cdots+v_{\pi}f_{r-1}<A
, so the local functional equation and rationality statement hold for the series
D_{\pi}(x,\vec{f}_{\pi};M)
. Therefore by Lemma
4.5
, the global functional equation and rationality statement hold for
\vec{f}
. This establishes the global functional equation and rationality statement for all
\vec{f}
except when the
f_{i}
are all powers of the same linear polynomial.

We now consider the sum

\sum_{\deg f_{1}=d_{1},\ldots\deg f_{r-1}=d_{r-1}}D(x,\vec{f})-\sum_{\deg\pi=1}D(x,(\pi^{d_{1}},\ldots\pi^{d_{r-1}})).

(4.18)

Set
v=d_{1}M_{1r}+\cdots d_{r-1}M_{(r-1)\,r}
. We know that this sum is a rational function with denominator dividing
1-qx
if
n|v
